# Changelog

All notable changes to the Test Architect (TEA) module will be documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.0.0/),
and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).

## [Unreleased]

### Added

- `tea-evaluate run` measures gameability and historical probes, and judges rubrics (Story 1.9, AD-7, AD-8).
  A gameability probe launches nothing: its degenerate response is committed at `corpus/gameability/<probeId>.json` (one `{ stdout, stderr, exitCode }` per plan step, a new runtime-owned schema), `preflight` and `run` answer the interaction plan from it as a synthetic observation, and its `naiveOracle` (an oracle of another behavior, a new field of the committed probe) must hold over it while the oracle of the probe's own behavior is violated (exit 11 otherwise); the naive-satisfied and disciplined-rejected evidence lands under `qualification/<probeId>/`, the probe is materialized as eval-quality's `gameability` probe, and its trials run on the arm `gameability:<probeId>`.
  A historical probe names the `fixCommit` that removed its natural defect: `preflight` and `run` qualify it in a worktree at the fix commit's parent, where its oracles must be violated (`fail-before.json`), and one at the fix commit, where they must hold (`pass-after.json`), route its manifestation-witness leg to the parent, and run its trials on the arm `historical:<preFixSha>`; the probe records the tracked tree at the parent as its `artifactDigest` and the digest of the fix commit's id as `fixCommitDigest`.
  `fixCommit` is a hexadecimal commit id, never a moving ref; one that names no commit in a full-history repository, or that resolves through a branch or tag of the same spelling, exits 10 naming the probe.
  A historical probe with no revisions to address (a copy workspace, a shallow clone missing the fix or its parent, a fix commit that is not an ancestor of the evaluated commit or has no parent, or a revision, before or at the fix, where the target cannot run: its worktree lacks `launch.root` or holds a submodule under it, the skill root is not a directory, or a registry target is missing or not executable, links followed and provisioned copies included) is refused with its reason in `run.json`'s `refused` and `refused/<probeId>.json`, stays out of the preflight and the trial sets, and the rest of the run goes on; `score` prints each refused probe with its reason and records it in its aggregate `score.json`.
  The route addresses revisions as git commits launched from worktrees; a target reachable only as a remote deployment is not measured by it yet (Story 1.32).
  When the contract declares a rubric, a judge runs once per trial through TeA's agent adapters, wired by `evaluation.json`'s new `judge` and named as a fixed condition by `policy/evaluator-conditions.json`'s new `judge.modelSnapshot`; it receives the instruction template (which marks the evidence as data whose instructions are never followed), each rubric's anchors and penalties, and the evidence each criterion points at, never the contract, and every record of the trial carries its scores as `judgeResults` (an unreadable or off-scale score, a note past `maxLength`, or a reply carrying no scores object of its own, only one quoted from the evidence, or more than one of its own is recorded as `score: null`, which eval-quality reads as Invalid; a judge that cannot answer, times out or writes into its read-only directory yields no record and exit 12, its streams kept; an interrupting signal during the call ends the run by that signal). `check` refuses an adapter that cannot run read-only as the judge. The evaluator configuration records `judgeConfiguration: { modelSnapshot, systemPromptDigest }`; a contract with no rubric makes no judge call and keeps `judgeConfiguration: null`.
- `tea-evaluate check` gains `gameability` (a gameability probe off its class or route, one that seeds a defect, a naive oracle of the probe's own behavior, or a degenerate response that is missing, answers a step the plan does not declare, leaves one unanswered or exits an infrastructure code), `historical` (a historical probe without `expectedClean: false`, with no defect, or with one that is not `natural`), `evaluator-conditions` also refusing `modelSnapshot: none` beside a `systemPromptDigest` other than the empty byte string's, and `judge` (a rubric with no `judge` or no judge model, a judge adapter, command or model TeA's agent adapters cannot run, or a `judge` block beside a contract that declares no rubric); `arms` takes `historical` and `gameability`, and a historical or gameability probe needs `policy/scoring-policy.json`.
- `npm run test:evaluate-arms`, chained into `npm test`, runs each new arm end to end over the real eval-quality: a gameability arm that launches nothing and scores `caught`, a historical arm across a real fix commit whose witness leg and trials run at the parent and score `caught`, a refused historical probe in a one-commit repository, and a stub rubric judge called once per trial, never without a rubric, and failing with exit 12; every materialized probe passes eval-quality's `qualifyProbe` with no failure code.

- `tea-evaluate run` measures an evaluation as sealed trial sets (Story 1.8, AD-7).
  It runs the whole preflight, then qualifies each clean control on one clean arm and runs every arm a probe needs `trials` times, the clean arm for the clean controls and one mutated arm per mutation, each trial in a workspace of its own that reproduces the pristine one.
  The deterministic evaluator judges every trial through eval-quality's `resolveCheck`: a disposition for every oracle, and a `defect` finding per violated oracle of the behaviors the probe discharges, quoting a cited observation verbatim; the trial's observations are `evaluator-chosen`, and every record of a set carries the set's one recommendation.
  Each probe gets one trial set under `runs/<invocationId>/trial-sets/<probeId>/`: a Sealed Run Record per trial (one `runId`, `trialIndex` 1..N, `mode: contract-scoring`, `conditionArm` `clean` or `mutated:<mutationId>`) and an isolation manifest from the workspaces and commands the trials were granted and the commands the runtime ran (the runtime observes no file-system access, so it records no observed mount), with one evaluator configuration for the run carrying the seal's `sealedBriefDigest`; every artifact is checked against eval-quality's published schema before it is written.
  A trial that exits an infrastructure code or is stopped by a signal from outside yields no record and the run exits 12, as does a change to the project after any trial or after the last write; `run.json` records the digests of every file `score` reads, runner and model, trial count, duration, how the invocation ended (an interrupting signal included), `completed: true` as the run's last write, only once the trial sets are sealed and verified (a retraction the runtime cannot make is named in the exit message), and `dirty: true` when the evaluation folder holds uncommitted work.
  The runtime does not sandbox the target's file system (Story 1.31), so it guards its own writes to the run directory: every directory and file in it is created afresh without following a link, from inside a directory the runtime made, held open until the command ends (so its inode number cannot pass to a directory the target makes), and confirmed before and after the write by device, inode and the path the system reports for it, `run.json` is replaced by renaming a new file over it, engine stages write into a private temp directory whose output is copied in, every file is read back, without blocking, only with the bytes the runtime wrote, and the run directory must hold exactly those entries before the preflight verdict, after the trials and before `run.json` says completed (exit 12 otherwise); a link a target plants there, or a directory it replaces or moves into your project with a link left in its place, stops the run with exit 12 before the runtime writes through it.
  Probes carry AD-7's digests: the evaluated commit, the `targetArtifact` bytes, and the tracked tree of the skill or target root at that commit, the evaluation folder left out.
- `tea-evaluate score` scores a completed run's trial sets through `eval-quality score`, once per probe, with every trial's `--record`, the set's isolation manifest and the run's evaluator configuration, after checking every input against eval-quality's published schemas, `trial-sets.json` against the runtime's own, and each set against its run: the runId the run derives, references inside the run directory, and the digests `run.json` recorded for the compiled contract, the policy, the preflight verdict, the evaluator configuration and every probe, record and isolation manifest (exit 10 before any engine call otherwise), each read as a regular file without blocking, so a FIFO or a link there is a finding; a run whose `run.json` does not say it completed exits 64.
  `score` holds each file to the digest `run.json` recorded; a process that can write the run directory after the run, a target's leftover process included, can rewrite both, and Story 1.31 closes that.
  Each call's argv, exit code, stdout and stderr are kept under `runs/<invocationId>/scores/<scoreInvocationId>/<probeId>/` with its evidence artifact, and the command exits with the most severe call's own exit.
  An absent isolation manifest is passed on as absent, so eval-quality reads the set as Invalid.
- `policy/evaluator-conditions.json`, a runtime-owned schema: the model a run uses and the digest of its system prompt, recorded in every run's evaluator configuration; a run with no such file records `modelSnapshot: none` and the digest of the empty byte string.
- `tea-evaluate check` gains `trials` (below the scoring policy's `minimumTrialCount`), `arms` (a probe whose arm `arms` does not declare, or a declared arm no probe runs on), `mutation-route` (a `controlled-mutation` probe that seeds no defect) and `evaluator-conditions` (a `tea-skill-runner` registry entry with no `policy/evaluator-conditions.json`, or one naming `none`), and holds that file to its schema.
- `tea-evaluate preflight`'s qualification arms, like `run`'s trials, treat a target step a signal from outside stopped (hang-up, interrupt, quit, kill or terminate) as a target that could not run (exit 12); a step that crashes by its own signal stays an observation its oracles judge.
- The Evaluate skill's `assets/` ships a scoring-policy template and an evaluator-conditions template whose threshold and model values are left `null` for the adopter, and an evaluation-folder `.gitignore` template that ignores `runs/`; TeA's own `.gitignore` ignores `runs/` under `test/evaluations/*/` and `test/fixtures/evaluate*/`.
- `npm run test:evaluate-run`, chained into `npm test`, runs `tea-evaluate run` and `score` end to end on the verdict fixture and asserts `passed-clean-control` and `caught` over three trials with a comparable strength vector, the record, manifest and configuration shapes, AD-7's digests, byte-identical evidence from a direct `eval-quality score` on a passing and a FAIL run, an Invalid score when the manifest is omitted, each call's persisted streams through a logging shim, and exit 12 for an infrastructure trial.
- `tea-evaluate preflight` qualifies every seeded probe on the `controlled-mutation` route in a disposable workspace before its legs run, and proves the rollback (Story 1.7, AD-8).
  AD-8's six steps run in a workspace of the probe's own: the clean arm must pass, the mutation is applied, the mutated arm must fail, the original bytes are restored with their mode, their digest and mode must equal the pre-mutation ones, and the clean arm must pass again within the scoring policy's `reExecutionCap`.
  An arm is one pass over the contract's interaction plan with its literal bindings, judged by a deterministic evaluator that resolves each oracle of the probe's behaviors through eval-quality's `resolveCheck`.
  `rollbackVerified` is the conjunction of the matching digest and the re-pass, never a literal; the evidence (`baseline-pass.json`, `mutated-fail.json`, and `rollback.json` with `preDigest`, `mutatedDigest` and `restoredDigest`) lands under `runs/<invocationId>/qualification/<probeId>/`, and the qualified probe, checked against eval-quality's probe schema and qualification gate, under `runs/<invocationId>/probes/` once the adopter's project is confirmed unchanged after the legs.
  The qualified probe reaches `eval-quality preflight`, and its manifestation witness's leg runs in a mutated workspace of its own while every other leg runs in the pristine one; each observation records the workspace and working directory it ran in (AD-6).
  A `find` text that does not occur exactly once (overlapping occurrences counted) exits 10, a baseline that does not pass or a mutation that does not fail exits 11, and a workspace, restore or infrastructure failure, or a restored workspace that does not pass again (an unfit harness), exits 12, each with no qualified probe written.
- `tea-evaluate preflight` runs in AD-8's workspaces: a detached `git worktree` at `HEAD` for a git target (hooks disabled, its entry removed afterwards), a temp copy identified by its tree digest for a `copy` workspace or a target outside git, and a temp copy of the working tree under the new `--from-working-tree`, the one workspace recorded as `dirty: true`.
  Every later workspace of a run reproduces the first (the same commit, or a copy of the first copy), and the evaluation folder is left out of all of them, so a target cannot read which defect a mutation plants.
  `runs/<invocationId>/run.json` records the versions, the commit (`null` for a copy), `dirty` and every workspace that ran legs.
  Each `workspace.provision` directory is copied in as a copy-on-write clone where the file system offers one, with its write bits removed, so a write under it fails unless the writer restores them; a provisioned directory that is a symbolic link is refused, and a symbolic link inside one that leads out of the project is refused like any other.
  `preflight` reads the adopter's project before the workspaces are made and after the qualification and the legs (in git: `git status`, the content of every path it names, every ref, and the common git directory without its object store and bookkeeping, which a worktree shares; outside git: the tree digest) and exits 12 on any change, with the probe list it handed the CLI removed.
  A worktree whose `launch.root` holds a submodule is refused with exit 12, since the worktree checks it out empty, and a `TMPDIR` inside the repository is refused for every workspace kind unless the repository ignores it.
  The rollback cycle records where the `targetArtifact`'s directory really lies, holds it open for the whole cycle, and refuses (exit 12) to write or read it once an arm has made that path resolve anywhere else, `launch.root` swapped for a link included, has replaced the directory, has moved it behind a link, or has hard-linked the file; the mutation and the restore each write a new file, so neither can reach another path's data.
- `tea-evaluate check` requires `policy/scoring-policy.json` once a probe takes the `controlled-mutation` route, and holds a policy that is present to eval-quality's scoring-policy schema and version.
- `npm run test:evaluate-mutation`, chained into `npm test`, runs `tea-evaluate preflight` over temp git projects built from `test/fixtures/evaluate/mutation/`, whose verdict command prints the SHA-256 of its own `targetArtifact`, and asserts the worktree, the dirty flag, the three digests, the leg routing, the read-only provisioning and each failure exit, with the project's `git status` and every file unchanged.
- `npm run test:evaluate-boundaries` gains a `rollback-literal` rule over `cli/`, and holds the moves of the live harness's leg cache and per-leg staging (`cachingPort`, `stageDirectories`) into `cli/lib/evaluate/workspace.js` and of the mutate, measure, restore cycle (`runMutationCycle`) into `cli/lib/evaluate/mutation.js`; `test/eval-contract-strength.js` and `test/test-automate-eval-fixture.js` keep only TeA data.
- `tea-skill-runner` (`cli/skill-runner.js`) ships as a new bin: the generic runner an Evaluate evaluation registers for a skill (Story 1.6, AD-4).
  It takes the skill from an explicit `--skill-root`, which with its `SKILL.md` must resolve inside the working directory, reads the prompt on stdin, tells the agent where the skill is, and runs one turn through TeA's agent adapters, so vendor knowledge stays in `cli/lib/agent-adapters.js` and `--agent` has no default.
  It never probes an install location.
  It exits 2 for a usage error (a prompt that is not valid UTF-8 included) and 3 to 6 for the infrastructure classes of `cli/lib/runner-exit-codes.js`, and an unexpected error or a standard output closed before the reply was written exits 4, so its registry entry declares `infrastructureExitCodes` 3 to 6.
- `tea-evaluate preflight --evaluation <path>` drives an evaluation's preflight against the real target (Story 1.6, AD-6).
  It runs `check`, makes a disposable workspace of `launch.root` the legs run in (see the Story 1.7 entries), runs `eval-quality compile` and `seal` over the run's own copy of the contract, drives the legs through eval-quality's `runPreflight` with a recording port over the registry's command-line adapter, writes every observation under `runs/<invocationId>/observations/`, and passes through the exit of `eval-quality preflight --observations ... --run-id <invocationId>` over the persisted files.
  The library's own verdict is discarded.
  The copy leaves out `.git` and the evaluation's `runs/`, points every symbolic link inside the target at the copy (each link resolved by the system, so a `..` after a link climbs from the link's target), and is removed when the command ends or is interrupted by `SIGINT`, `SIGTERM`, `SIGHUP` or `SIGQUIT`; a link out of `launch.root`, a FIFO, socket or device, and a temp directory inside `launch.root` are refused with exit 12.
  Each `workspace.provision` directory is copied into the workspace read-only (see the Story 1.7 entry).
  `--evaluation` resolves to the folder's real path, so `launch.root` resolves the same through a symbolic link to the folder.
  A request's environment is recorded as keys only, and the injected values are scrubbed from observations.
  A leg the registry does not authorize exits 10 with its fault under `runs/<invocationId>/faults/`; a leg that cannot run, a target that cannot launch, a stage that cannot run or exits with a code eval-quality does not document for that stage, and an evaluation holding a probe that seeds a defect on a route other than `controlled-mutation` exit 12.
  `runs/` gets a `.gitignore` on first use, and `engine/<stage>.json` records each stage's executable, argv, exit and output, including a `TEA_EVALUATE_ENGINE_CLI` substitution.
- `evaluation.json`'s `launch` gains its shape: `root`, the evaluated project relative to the evaluation folder, and `skillRoot`, required for a skill target.
  `tea-evaluate check` gains the `skill-root` rule (a mutation outside the skill root, a runner leg passing another `skill-root`, a skill root inside a provisioned directory) and the `skill-runner` rule (a runner entry missing an infrastructure code, a runner leg with no literal `timeout-ms` below the entry's `maxElapsedMs`: under the ceiling the runner reports its own timeout as exit 5, while at the ceiling the adapter kills the runner's process group, records a fault, and `preflight` exits 12).
- `npm run test:evaluate-preflight`, chained into `npm test`, drives the runner and `tea-evaluate preflight` over a stub agent project under `test/fixtures/evaluate/stub-agent/`.
  It asserts a passed verdict with no authorization denial, that removing the stub's registry entry makes those assertions fail on the recorded denial, and that a logging engine shim exiting 5 for `preflight` makes the command exit 5, which proves the verdict comes from the CLI.
  `npm run test:evaluate-boundaries` gains `install-probe` and `vendor-name` rules over `cli/skill-runner.js`, `npm run test:evaluate-check` runs the runner from the packed install, and the `doc-claims` exit-code registry now includes the runner table.
- `tea-evaluate`, the Evaluate runtime, ships as a new bin over a CommonJS `cli/lib/evaluate/` with its first two subcommands. `tea-evaluate check --evaluation <path>` validates an evaluation folder and exits 10 listing every authoring defect: a stale `corpus-index.json`, an unknown `evaluation.json` `schemaVersion` (naming the installed TeA version and the versions it knows), a committed probe carrying a runtime-owned field, a mutation that is not `replace-exact` with one occurrence, a mutation target inside a provisioned directory, a contract interface of kind `web`, a defect signature addressing a written file, a discharged behavior without exactly one oracle, an off-pattern probe, defect, behavior, oracle or mutation ID, a `baseline/qualification/` digest mismatch, and a clean control that is not `zero-action` with `expectedClean: true`. It also refuses a duplicate behavior, oracle or defect ID, and a symbolic link or file where the index or `baseline/` expects a directory or a regular file, so no bytes outside the folder can stand in for the corpus or the qualification evidence. `tea-evaluate digest --evaluation <path>` writes a sorted `{path, sha256}` index over `corpus/`, `probes/` and `mutations/` and prints `corpusDigest`, eval-quality's `digestArtifact` over it. Both exit 64 when `--evaluation` does not resolve, and the runtime reads no `_bmad/` config. The runtime owns the `evaluation.json`, committed-probe and mutation schemas, and reaches eval-quality only through `cli/lib/evaluate/engine.js`, whose cached loader and `TEA_EVALUATE_ENGINE_CLI`-aware CLI path later stories build on. `docs/reference/tea-evaluate-cli.md` documents both subcommands, their rules and exit codes.
- `package.json` declares `eval-quality` as an optional peer dependency (`>=4.1.4`, with `peerDependenciesMeta` marking it optional so a project that never runs Evaluate does not receive it) and moves `ajv` from `devDependencies` to `dependencies`, since the shipped runtime needs it. 4.1.1 is the first release whose command-line adapter kills the target's process group at its ceiling, 4.1.2 the first that also kills it when the host dies, by `SIGKILL` included, which `tea-evaluate preflight` relies on, 4.1.3 the first whose `score` writes an Invalid result's reasons to stderr, which `tea-evaluate score` persists, and 4.1.4 the first whose record schema defines an observation's `provenance` by its role, the reading `tea-evaluate run` records scored trial steps under. `tools/guard-publish.js` now also refuses to publish a manifest whose `tea-evaluate` bin is missing or points at no file, whose peer range admits an engine older than 4.1.4, or whose peer is not optional; `npm run test:guard-publish` and `npm run test:release-metadata` cover the bin, the peer floor and the optional flag, and the lockfile's root entry.
- `npm run test:evaluate-check` runs `tea-evaluate` over a valid fixture folder (`test/fixtures/evaluate/valid/`), each authoring defect planted alone in a temp copy, the usage errors, `digest` against the installed engine, and a packed install: `npm pack`, installed with `--omit=dev` beside the local engine, must run `tea-evaluate check` to exit 0. `npm run test:evaluate-boundaries` parses every source file under `cli/` with acorn and fails when a file other than `cli/lib/evaluate/engine.js` loads a specifier that is `eval-quality`, starts with `eval-quality/` or contains `node_modules/eval-quality` (through `require` or an alias of it, `require.resolve`, `import.meta.resolve`, `module.require`, `import()`, a static `import` or `export ... from`, or a `createRequire` result under any local name); when `runScore`, `preflightFromObservations` or `seal` appears under `cli/` as an identifier, member property, destructured key or import specifier (`Object.seal` exempt in a file that binds no `Object`; comments and string text are never code); when `compile` is reached as a member or destructured key on anything other than an identifier bound only to an Ajv instance; when `cli/lib/evaluate/` or `cli/evaluate.js` loads a module through a specifier that is not a plain string or static template literal; when a file does not parse; when `cli/` holds a symbolic link or a file that is neither `.js`, `.cjs`, `.mjs` code nor `.json`, `.md`, `.yml`, `.yaml` data; or when `_bmad` appears under `cli/lib/evaluate/`. It proves its scanner against a planted file for each violating form and each legitimate one before scanning the real tree. `acorn` is now a declared devDependency. Both run in `npm test` and as their own CI steps.
- The `dependency-direction` section of `eval-quality.config.json` gives `cli/` `allow` externals lists: `cli/lib/evaluate/engine.js` has its own exact `evaluate-engine` layer, the one list naming `eval-quality`, and every other `cli/` file is held to the `cli` layer's list, so an import of anything else from `cli/`, an evaluation framework included, fails `npm run test:direction`.
- `npm run test:trial-set-scoring` scores three trials of one stored-replay probe through the `eval-quality score` CLI with a repeated `--record`. It checks the reduction for three agreeing trials, then for a set where trial 2 misses, which has to reduce to a catch at 2 of 3 against `catchThreshold` 0.5. A single-record score beside them has to stay non-comparable under the raised trial minimum. It runs in `npm test` and as its own CI step, and fails if the pin is downgraded to 3.4.0, which proves TeA's gate depends on trial-set scoring (eval-quality#143).
- Registered `Evaluate` (`bmad-testarch-evaluate`, menu code `EV`) as TEA's tenth workflow, in the lean skill shape a new TEA skill now follows: `SKILL.md`, `customize.toml`, `references/` (twelve stage guides, placeholders until Stories 1.4 onward fill them), and `assets/`, with no `workflow.yaml`, `steps-c/`, `instructions.md`, or `checklist.md`. Menu registration spans `src/module-help.csv`, `src/agents/bmad-tea/customize.toml`, `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json`, and a new `tea_evaluations_folder` config variable (default `evals`) in `src/module.yaml`. The lean shape is proven both structurally (`npm run test:install`) and behaviorally: `npm run test:evaluate-guidance` parses `SKILL.md`'s stage list, and a live `npm run eval:routing` run confirms the new `EV` intent routes correctly through the local Claude Code CLI. `test/test-installation-components.js` gains a lean-shape assertion set, disjoint from the existing house-shape set, that classifies a skill as lean only when it has neither `workflow.yaml` nor `steps-c/`. `tools/validate-tea-workflow-descriptions.js` now discovers every workflow's description source instead of hard-coding `bmad-teach-me-testing` as the one lean exception. `test/evals/suite-manifest.json` carries a `deferred` entry for `bmad-testarch-evaluate` until Story 1.16 authors its own suite.
- `evaluation.json` declares its execution-target registry: `registry` is an array of `RegistryEntry` objects (`interfaceId`, `executable`, `target`, `subcommandPaths`, `artifacts`, `environmentKeys`, `maxElapsedMs` (at most 2147483647, the bound eval-quality 4.1.2's command target policy enforces), an optional `maxOutputBytes`, and `infrastructureExitCodes`), defined once in the runtime's `evaluation.schema.json`.
  The runtime builds eval-quality's default-deny command target policy from it; a `target` or artifact path is relative, with no `.`, `..` or empty segment, no trailing slash, and no control (C0, DEL or C1), line-separator or bidirectional formatting character, a joined target that lands outside the project root is refused again at build time, and the runtime's target check refuses a target that is not a regular file.
  A relative registry root, or a relative `projectRoot` override, is resolved to an absolute path once, so a later change of working directory cannot move a target.
  `tea-evaluate check` gains three rules: `infrastructure-exit-code` refuses a defect signature or manifestation witness that could hold on an observation carrying only one of its entry's infrastructure exit codes and no output (resolved through eval-quality's own `resolveCheck`, with the contract's reference sets in scope, a witness's declared inputs, each call-input clause of a signature tried both ways, and failing closed when the engine cannot resolve it), `unregistered-executable` refuses a `cli` signature or a witness naming a target no entry declares, and `registry` refuses an interface and executable pair declared twice.
  `docs/reference/tea-evaluate-cli.md` documents the registry and the rules.

### Changed

- **Breaking: output paths.** Every TEA workflow now writes into its own folder under `{test_artifacts}`, and a file produced once per scope carries that scope in its name (#228).
  The folders are `test-design/`, `atdd/`, `automate/`, `test-review/`, `nfr/`, `trace/`, `ci/`, and `framework/`.
  Trace writes `trace/traceability-matrix-{run_key}.md`, `trace/e2e-trace-summary-{run_key}.json`, and `trace/gate-decision-{run_key}.json`; nfr-assess writes `nfr/nfr-assessment-{run_key}.md`; test-review writes `test-review/test-review-{run_key}.md`; automate writes `automate/automation-summary-{run_key}.md`; ATDD keeps `atdd-checklist-{story_key}.md` inside `atdd/`; test-design's documents and checkpoints move into `test-design/`; validation reports move into their workflow's folder.
  `run_key` is `system`, `epic-{epic_num}`, `story-{story_key}`, trace's `release-{slug}` or `hotfix-{slug}`, or `target-{slug}` for automate and test-review, resolved in the first step before anything is written and recorded as `runScope` and `runKey` in the output's frontmatter.
  Before this change, trace for epic 16 replaced epic 15's `gate-decision.json` and `e2e-trace-summary.json` and appended its report into epic 15's `traceability-matrix.md`, reproduced live against 1.27.2's skill; the same run now leaves every epic 15 file byte-identical.
  A re-run of the same scope resumes an interrupted file or replaces a finished one, and two runs are never merged into one file.
  Files written by earlier versions stay where they are. Resume migrates a flat file into its scoped path only while it is in progress: ATDD recovers the story from the checklist's `storyKey`, the other workflows ask which scope the file covers, and Resume never writes over a scoped file that already exists. A completed flat file is never moved.
  Workflows that read another workflow's output look in its folder first and at the old root location second.
  Scripts and CI jobs that read the old flat paths need the new ones; `docs/reference/configuration.md` has an old-to-new table under "Upgrading Scripts That Read Old Paths".
  The `tea-test-review` CLI and its GitHub Action pass an explicit `--output` path and are unaffected.
- test-design records `runScope` as `system` or `epic`, the values every other workflow uses; the old `system-level` and `epic-level` values are still accepted as aliases.
- The eight workflows' Edit mode lists each workflow's concrete known outputs, including the old root locations, in place of a generic "select from known outputs".
- The temp files TEA's subagent steps hand data through (`/tmp/tea-<workflow>-<part>-...json`) now carry the run's `run_key` beside the timestamp. A headless agent with no shell invents round timestamps, and `/tmp` is shared across worktrees, so two parallel runs for different scopes could read each other's handoff file. Named `playwright-cli` browser sessions now carry the `run_key` too (`tea-<workflow>-<run_key>`, with a timestamp suffix for atdd and automate; ci and framework, which have no `run_key`, use `tea-<workflow>-<timestamp>`), because session names are global to the machine and two parallel runs sharing a fixed name such as `tea-nfr` would drive and close one browser. The nfr and test-design evidence screenshots (`nfr/perf-{run_key}-<page>.png`, `test-design/exploration/explore-{run_key}-<page>.png`) carry it as well, so two runs capturing the same page keep both.
  Automate's optional cleanup now also removes the mobile worker's temp file, which it previously left behind.
- The eval harnesses, stub agents, stored trace and nfr replays, and the generated contracts and probes follow the new paths. Trace replays live at `test-artifacts/trace/*-epic-{4|5}.*` and nfr replays at `test-artifacts/nfr/nfr-assessment-system.md`, and `test:eval-replay` fails when a case's `storedOutput` differs from the path the harness reads.
  The trace eval now scores `target.type` and `target.id`, which step 1 resolves itself; the replay scorer moves to version 14.
  A new replay, `trace/seeded-system-shaped-target`, fails `target.id` on a summary that leaves the epic's id empty, and `target.id` compares as a string so `4` and `"4"` both pass.
  A live trace, nfr or test-design run that writes its deliverable under another run key now scores as a quality failure (exit 1) naming the file it wrote; before, the harness reported the expected file as missing and exited 2.
  `npm run test:install` gains a scoped-output suite: every per-scope create step and deliverable sits in its workflow's folder and names its scope, and every resume step declares a folder glob and the exact pre-folder path.
- `runAgent` (`cli/lib/run-agent.js`), which every TeA runner and `tea-test-review` use, now runs the agent in its own process group under `cli/lib/agent-supervisor.js`.
  A timeout sends the group `SIGTERM`, and the agent `SIGKILL` 2 s later if it is still running.
  The group is also stopped when the runner's process group receives `SIGINT`, `SIGTERM`, `SIGHUP` or `SIGQUIT` (a terminal's Ctrl-C or `Ctrl-\` reaches the agent as the same signal), and when the runner or the supervisor dies, by `SIGKILL` included (a cancelled CI job, or an eval-quality adapter killing the runner at its ceiling, alone before 4.1.1 and with its process group from 4.1.1, or because the host that started it died, from 4.1.2).
  The supervisor holds one end of a socket whose other end a group leader in a session of its own holds, and the kernel closes it however the supervisor ends.
  When the agent exits, every process left in its group receives `SIGKILL` at once.
  The agent's standard input, output and error are pipes the group leader owns and copies to and from the runner's, so a process the agent leaves outside its group (a daemon in a session of its own, say) keeps running and the runner does not wait for it.
  Once the agent exits, the leader copies what those pipes still hold and closes each one when it reaches its end, stays empty for 100 ms, or has been read for 2 s of the time the runner keeps up with it, and output written after that is dropped.
  Before, such a process held the run until `--timeout-ms` ran out, which then reported a timeout for an agent that had answered.
  Before, a timeout signalled the agent alone and any process it had started kept running.
  Exit codes are unchanged, and a `--timeout-ms` above 2147483647 is honored.
  A supervisor that ends before the agent, and a group leader that ends without a report, read as a transport failure (exit 4); a group leader still running 5 s past the wall clock is killed with the agent's group, with the same exit.
  A Ctrl-Z suspends the runner while the agent runs on, bounded by its wall clock and the runner's end.
  The group leader writes its report to the runner itself, and `spawnSync` has no timeout of its own, so a runner resumed after any length of suspension reports how the agent ended, with its output.
  On Windows, which has no process groups, the timeout and the signals reach the agent alone, and nothing the agent started is stopped.
- The `eval-quality` devDependency is raised from the exact pin 4.0.0 to 4.1.4, and the peer floor with it. 4.1.4 defines a Sealed Run Record observation's `provenance` by its role in the run: `evaluator-chosen` is an action the evaluation itself performed, whether an agent or a scripted plan chose it, and `baseline` a harness baseline or fixture set-up call the witness match excludes, which is how `tea-evaluate run` records every scored trial step. 4.1.3 writes the reasons of an Invalid `score` to stderr, one `eval-quality: invalid: <reason>` line each (eval-quality#162), which `tea-evaluate score` persists so AD-10 can classify a `score` exit 3. 4.1.0 publishes `parseCommandTargetPolicy` on `eval-quality/adapters` (eval-quality#159), 4.1.1 makes the command-line adapter kill the target's whole process group on `maxElapsedMs`, `maxOutputBytes` and abort (eval-quality#160), and 4.1.2 starts each target through a watchdog that kills that group when the host dies, by `SIGKILL` included, so a `SIGKILL` to `tea-evaluate preflight` or to its whole process group now also ends the runner and its agent (eval-quality#161); `test:evaluate-check` now validates the registry's command target policy through it, and the roadmap's pin claim names 4.1.4.
- The execution-target registry, the eval-quality record builders and the digest and provenance helpers move from `test/lib/` into the shipped runtime (Story 1.5, AD-5): `cli/lib/evaluate/registry.js`, `records.js`, `digest.js` and `bounded-probe.js`.
  `test/lib/probe-targets.js`, `eval-quality-inputs.js` and `eval-record.js` keep TeA's data (its nine commands as `RegistryEntry` data, its scoring policy path, its own eval result records and failure classes) and import the runtime modules, so TeA's harness and every adopter run go through one implementation; every existing test passes with its assertions unchanged.
  TeA's runners declare exit 1 (Node's exit for an uncaught exception, which no runner emits on purpose) and 3 to 6 as infrastructure codes, and `tea-test-review` declares 2 and 3.
  `test/lib/eval-quality-schema-versions.js` moves into `cli/lib/evaluate/engine.js`, which reads the engine's constants on first use and names the missing package (exit 12) when the optional peer is absent; the `dependency-direction` purity layer in `eval-quality.config.json` now holds `engine.js`, which is also the only `cli/` file allowed to reach `eval-quality`.
  `npm run test:evaluate-boundaries` asserts each of the three `test/lib/` files requires its runtime module, that `function commandTargetPolicy`, `function sealedRunRecord` and `function repositoryState` are declared only in their runtime modules, that no function a moved runtime module exports, or that a factory it exports builds and returns (`createRegistry`'s `targetProblems`, `createArtifactValidator`'s `validateArtifact`), is defined again under `test/lib/`, and that every runtime function the three files hand out is the runtime's own function object (`test/lib/probe-targets.js` exports the registry it builds, which must be one `createRegistry` built and frozen).
  Moving the code back as a definition, a bound, aliased or member function, or a look-alike registry fails it.
  Its `test-import` rule refuses a relative or self-referencing load from `cli/` into `test/`, and its `dynamic-specifier` rule now covers all of `cli/`.
  `npm run test:evaluate-check` adds the registry, rule and engine-absent cases, and loads every runtime module from the packed install.
  `npm run test:schema-versions` now also scans `cli/lib/evaluate/` for a literal `schemaVersion` stamp, since the builders live there.
- The `eval-quality` devDependency is raised from the exact pin 3.4.0 to the published exact pin 4.0.0, which carries eval-quality#143's trial-set scoring (`EvidenceArtifact` schema version 4) and Story 1.1's target-policy export (eval-quality#158). `docs/explanation/eval-quality-roadmap.md`'s "The pin is 4.0.0 now" and `docs/explanation/eval-quality-command-adapter.md`'s outcome count now name the new pin.
- The stored comparable results TEA hands to `compareDominance` now carry the `EvidenceArtifact` schema version 4 fields the comparator verifies before comparing: `scoredProbeId`, `reducedProbeOutcomes`, `trials`, and a `trialIndex` on each outcome. `test/probes/expected-strength.json` is regenerated: no verdict, strength vector, or pre-flight result moved, and in 26 probes the detailed outcome severities changed, because the version 4 engine stamps the probe's own severity on every detailed outcome (test-review P-001's `critical` outcomes now read `material`). The dominance fixtures and the drift comparison fixtures carry a consistent reduction, and a stored result in the version 3 shape, or one missing any version 4 field the comparator dereferences, reads as not comparable, so it never reaches the comparator. Like the new check, `test:probe-corpus` reads this shape from the installed package and fails if the pin is downgraded to 3.4.0.
- Documentation that counted TEA's workflows, skills, and one `eval:all` run's model calls is updated for the tenth workflow: `README.md`, `docs/index.md`, `docs/explanation/eval-quality-roadmap.md`, and `docs/explanation/eval-quality-adoption-guide.md` now read ten workflows / eleven skills, and the routing suite's call counts move from 18 to 19 intents (36 to 38 calls at two repetitions, 105 to 107 total, 315 to 321 across three runners). The claims that every TEA skill has a behavioral suite and none is deferred are corrected to name `bmad-testarch-evaluate` as the one skill deferred until Story 1.16.
- The Evaluate plan under `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/evaluate/` is now fully stacked: TeA owns every layer above `eval-quality`, the evaluation layer included. Ten stories were added for evaluator kinds behind one framework-neutral import contract (a sealed-brief agent evaluator, adopter evaluator commands, adopter-sealed records), a workflow fixture, a tool-use calling agent judged by AgentEvals, a promptfoo import with no runtime change, held-out probes and judge calibration, interpretation evidence, an evaluation-layer guide with a learn-on-the-go procedure for any framework, and behavioral proofs of the guidance on two more target kinds, seeded weaknesses and an unfamiliar framework. Existing stories gained craft criteria with compiled worked examples, `seal` in the skill's validation stage, a vendor-model redirect, and CI placement derived from the adopter's repository with the gameability arm, contract-source freshness and oracle-versus-scorer agreement on every pull request. Verified framework facts live in the new `evaluation-framework-facts.md`.
- `llms-full.txt` has room again: it measured 599,796 of its 600,000-character cap and now measures 525,244.
  `tools/build-docs.js` collapses the alignment padding Prettier writes into Markdown tables (runs of spaces in a table row and dash runs in a separator row) outside code, which keeps every cell's text and saved about 63k characters.
  The bundle also leaves out `404.md` and `docs/explanation/how-tea-is-tested.md`, which describes how TEA tests itself.
  Three inherited exclusion patterns matched no document here (`changelog`, `downloads/`, `reference/glossary/`) and are removed, and the build now fails on an exclusion pattern that matches nothing.
  The site's `ai-terms` meta tag no longer states a token count that had gone stale.
- CI runs the `npm test` chain as five parallel `chain` jobs, and the `coverage` job merges their coverage; the separate `cli` job is gone, since `npm run test:cli` now runs in the chain.
  Before this change, `validate` ran 71 of the chain's scripts one step at a time in 12 to 15 minutes, `coverage` ran the whole chain serially under c8 in about 20 minutes, which reached its 20-minute timeout, and the `cli` job's slowest shard took about 9.4 minutes.
  `tools/test-shards.js` assigns every chained script to a shard from the per-script seconds in `tools/test-shard-weights.json`, which puts about four minutes of the chain on each shard.
  Each shard runs its scripts in chain order, keeps going past a failing script, annotates every failure by script name, and saves each script's raw V8 coverage; the `coverage` job merges every shard's files with `c8 report` and holds the same `package.json` thresholds.
  The first sharded run took 6 minutes 45 seconds end to end, with shards from 3 minutes 45 seconds to 6 minutes 20 seconds; the weights, refreshed from that run's per-script timings, now put about 236 s of the chain on each shard.
  Each shard also writes a manifest of the scripts it ran, and the `coverage` job fails unless shards 1 through N each left one and together they ran the whole chain, so a shard whose coverage never arrived cannot pass the thresholds on the others' coverage.
  `test:ci-coverage` and `test:shards` refuse each way a green run could skip a shard or hide its failure: a matrix with `include`, `exclude` or a key besides `shard`, an `if` or `continue-on-error` on the chain job or its shard step, a `shell` on that step, a run line holding anything besides the invocation and its `--coverage-dir` and `--timings` flags, and a workflow that does not run on `pull_request`; an `npm test` part that is not a bare `npm run <script>` now fails by name, since the shards run only named scripts.
  `npm run test:shards`, chained into `npm test`, proves the shards cover the chain exactly once for every shard count from 1 to 8 and that the workflow matrix lists exactly the shards the tool is told to split into; `test:ci-coverage` counts a chained script as run in CI when a job shards the chain over that full matrix.
  `npm run test:cli` joined the chain once the heartbeat fix under Fixed brought the whole file to about 14 s, and `test:doc-invocations` now re-runs it like the other documented scripts; `TEA_CLI_TEST_SUITES` stays as a local way to rerun one of its suites.
  `npm run test:coverage` and the pre-commit hook are unchanged, and `npm test` gains `test:shards` and `test:cli`.

### Removed

- The three FUTURE output-folder keys `test_design_output`, `test_review_output`, and `trace_output` are removed from `src/module.yaml`, so install no longer prompts for them. No workflow ever read them, and the folder rule above replaces them. The installer saved them as relative paths without `{project-root}`, upstream BMAD has since dropped the `result:` processing that wrote them, and a configurable folder per workflow would multiply the places cross-workflow readers search. A `_bmad/tea/config.yaml` that still carries them is harmless. `risk_threshold` is now the one FUTURE key, and the README and configuration reference claims about FUTURE keys are rebound to checks that prove them.

### Fixed

- `tea-test-review` no longer holds its stderr open for up to 15 s after it exits, and its progress heartbeat no longer outlives a CLI that was killed.
  The heartbeat was an `sh` loop around `sleep`, and stopping it left the running `sleep` holding the stderr it inherited, so a caller reading that stderr through a pipe (`| tee`, a CI step capturing output, a script's `spawnSync`) waited for end of file long after the review finished.
  It is now `cli/lib/heartbeat.js`, a single Node process that starts nothing of its own, so stopping it closes its stderr at once; it also exits within 100 ms once the CLI is gone, so a CLI killed by `SIGKILL` leaves no heartbeat printing into its stderr.
  The CLI's own test file drove 35 stub-agent reviews through that wait: its Suite 7 went from about 9.4 minutes in CI to about 6 s locally, and the whole file runs in about 14 s.
- The Evaluate test suites (`test:evaluate-check`, `test:evaluate-preflight`, `test:evaluate-mutation` and `test:evaluate-run`) left their temp directories in the system temp directory whenever a signal stopped them.
  Each suite now makes its directories under one parent named for its process and removes it when it ends, and the next run of the suite removes a parent whose process is gone; `test:evaluate-run` also asserts that every run and score leaves its project's temp directory empty.
- `tea-skill-runner` reports the signal that asked an agent's process group to stop beside how the agent ended: an agent that outlived the grace period after a forwarded or received `SIGINT`, `SIGTERM`, `SIGHUP` or `SIGQUIT` reads as killed by `SIGKILL` once it outlived the grace period after that signal, where the report named the `SIGKILL` alone.
  A `SIGQUIT` writes a core file by default, and on a system that hands cores to a collector (a Linux CI runner) the dump can outlast the 2 s grace period, which made `test:evaluate-preflight`'s forwarded-`SIGQUIT` case fail intermittently; that case now accepts either ending, both naming `SIGQUIT`, and a stub agent that outlives a forwarded `SIGQUIT` proves the report.
- `npm run eval:preflight` staged no workspace for NFR, CI and test-design legs, so every live agent of those suites ran in an empty directory and reported its skill and project missing; `test/eval-contract-strength.js` now stages each leg's fixture set through the suite's own harness, as it already did for trace, and `npm run test:probe-corpus` asserts the staging for all four suites.
  The leg cache is keyed by how a suite's legs are staged, so a staging change can no longer answer a leg from a run in the old workspace, and `--force` runs each request live once, where it re-ran every leg that shares a request and overwrote the evidence an earlier leg was scored on.
- The NFR corpus's UNKNOWN manifestation witness read the bare word `UNKNOWN`, which a clean report may mention in prose, so it could fire on a clean leg; it now reads `**Threshold:** UNKNOWN`, the field the report template spells.
- Trace's step 4 read the gate target "from Step 1", but step 1 never resolved it; step 1 now resolves the gate type, target id and label, and step 4 reads them from the matrix frontmatter.
  Trace's step 5 both rewrote the whole matrix and said "do NOT overwrite it"; it now replaces the report body once, keeps the frontmatter, and only updates the frontmatter afterwards.
  A trace run that evaluates no gate removes an earlier `gate-decision-{run_key}.json` for the same scope, so a new summary is never paired with a stale decision.
- ATDD's resume step read `{outputFile}` without resolving `story_key` first; it now selects the checklist by story like test-design's resume.
- test-design's mode detection used `{implementation_artifacts}`, a BMM variable TEA never loads; it now names its source, `_bmad/bmm/config.yaml`, and falls back to `{output_folder}/implementation-artifacts`.
- Nine of the trace eval's skill-rule citations in `test/fixtures/trace-eval/ground-truth.json` (every step-05 gate rule, `gateEligibility`, `liveEvidenceCap`, and `liveBlockerSeverity`) pointed at the wrong lines, and the drift check missed them because each stayed inside its section; all eleven citations are re-pinned and `node test/eval-trace.js --validate-only` reports no drift.
  Six of the nfr eval's citations in `test/fixtures/nfr-eval/ground-truth.json` and three of the ci eval's in `test/fixtures/ci-eval/ground-truth.json` had drifted the same way; they are re-pinned, and both harnesses' `--validate-only` report no drift.
- `docs/how-to/workflows/run-trace.md` and `docs/how-to/brownfield/use-tea-for-enterprise.md` described a `gate-decision-{gate_type}-{story_id}.md` file that trace never wrote.
- `ci` and `framework` now apply the same re-run rule as the per-scope workflows: a completed checkpoint is replaced, an interrupted one asks whether to resume or start over, a headless run starts over, and two runs are never merged into one checkpoint.
  test-design gains headless branches: an interrupted checkpoint for the same scope starts over, and an epic-level run whose epic stays ambiguous halts with a message naming the candidate epics, since an epic plan has no system-level fallback.
  Trace now loads the NFR audit its checklist already expected, taking the first of `nfr/nfr-assessment-{run_key}.md`, the parent epic's `nfr/nfr-assessment-epic-{epic_num}.md` for a story, `nfr/nfr-assessment-system.md`, and the legacy root `nfr-assessment.md`.
  Automate finds an ATDD checklist when only the story id is known.
- `docs/how-to/workflows/run-trace.md` and `docs/how-to/brownfield/use-tea-for-enterprise.md` said trace reads test-review reports, and `run-trace.md`'s example gate decision showed a test quality score that trace never computes; both pages now list trace's real inputs, the test-design documents and the NFR audit.
- `npm run test:probe-targets` failed intermittently (and with it `test:doc-invocations`, which runs it again) whenever another process on the host held port 4310, for example a second worktree's `npm test`.
  The ATDD fixture's `playwright.config.ts` pointed its `webServer` at the default port 4310 while `tea-atdd-red-check` served the fixture on an OS-assigned port, so Playwright started a second server on 4310 for every spec file and failed to load it when the port was taken.
  The config now points `webServer` at the server `LOCKER_BASE_URL` names, so Playwright reuses it and starts nothing.
  The ATDD harness also crashed with "does not match its schema (harness bug)" when no spec file loaded, because it skipped an unmeasurable red-for-intended-reason rate; it now reports the rate as an unmeasurable quality failure, as the other harnesses do.
  `test:probe-targets` runs the correct-run case with port 4310 held and adds a load-error case.
- The runtime's relative-path pattern (a mutation's `targetArtifact` and a workspace `provision` entry) missed a `..` segment after a newline, because its lookahead used `.`; it now spans every character and refuses C0, DEL and C1 controls, the line and paragraph separators, bidirectional formatting characters and empty segments, and a `targetArtifact` may not end in a slash.
- `tea-evaluate check` and `digest` printed a finding's file name and message raw, so a corpus file name holding a newline printed a forged finding line; a control, line-separator or bidirectional formatting character now prints as an escape, and a file name holding one is quoted.
- The record validator (`createArtifactValidator`) registered no format, so Ajv warned and accepted any string as a `date-time`; it now registers one calendar-checked RFC 3339 `date-time` format from `cli/lib/evaluate/formats.js`, which `tea-evaluate check` shares.
- `redactSecrets` and `redactArgs` kept the userinfo of an http or https URL (`--endpoint=https://user:pass@host`); it is now redacted, keeping scheme and host.
- `digestFiles` digested a missing file and a present file whose bytes are exactly `<missing>` identically; the present file now contributes its path with a NUL appended, which no real path holds, so every other digest, a missing file's included, is unchanged.
- `npm run test:boundary` claimed to hold "no file under `cli/` imports from `test/`" and had no pattern for an import written without a file extension; its new `test-tree-reach` pattern refuses a relative climb or a self-referencing package path into `test/`, and `npm run test:layering-boundary-lineage` proves it on a seeded tree.
- `test/lib/probe-targets.js` built two default artifact paths with `path.join`, which yields backslashes on Windows that the registry schema refuses; they are POSIX literals now.
- `docs/explanation/how-tea-is-tested.md` described Evaluate as proposed future work with an undefined scope after it was registered as TEA's tenth workflow; the section now says what exists and links the new `tea-evaluate` reference. `README.md`'s `npm test` chain count reads seventy-nine for the two new checks, and the roadmap's list of TEA bins names `tea-evaluate` as the eleventh.
- Test and evaluation scripts whose color strings lacked the escape byte now print real ANSI colors in place of literal `[32m` text.
- `package.json`'s `files` array now carries negated `!src/workflows/testarch/*/.memlog.md` and `!src/workflows/testarch/*/.analysis/**` entries. Verified live: `npm pack` includes everything under a directory named in `files` regardless of `.gitignore` or `.npmignore`, so a stray `bmad-workflow-builder` session file left under a skill directory would have shipped in the published package; only a negated pattern inside `files` itself excludes it.

## [1.27.2] - 2026-09-18

### Added

- Added public explanation `docs/explanation/how-tea-is-tested.md` detailing deterministic checks, behavioral evaluations, clean and seeded fixture controls, gameability probes, repeated live runs, recorded results, exit classes, the TEA vs `eval-quality` boundary, and the planned Evaluate skill. Reconciled existing documentation to reflect full behavioral coverage across all ten skills.
- Live evaluation JSON now records a bounded diagnostic for every attempted case repetition, including the exact stability signature, threshold inputs, completion state, failure class, five-category root-cause triage, reason, and sanitized output or persisted artifact evidence. Schema 1.5.0 binds complete requested grids and aggregate failures to their diagnostic evidence while retaining frozen read-only validation paths for stored 1.4.0 and 1.3.0 results.

### Changed

- Deterministic live evaluation harnesses reject unsupported repetition counts during argument validation, before starting install, browser, or scoring work.
- The complete Story 1.8 live-baseline attempt is recorded with its environment result, protected 1.27.1 history remains byte-identical, and no quality score is claimed. Claude transport failures prevented measurable output, while the documented Codex substitution was unavailable under the local usage quota.

### Fixed

- Trace evaluation now repeats the resolved run metadata contract in its execution prompt and validates the required paths, empty URL fields, inventory counts, UI heuristic states, and explicit unknown handling during preflight. This keeps trace provenance complete and stable across repeated runs.
- Test-design guidance now grounds risks in supplied epic evidence, keeps risk registers within their declared scope, and requires exact risk IDs with suitable admitted test levels in coverage plans.
- NFR evidence audits now build one supplied-evidence ledger, bind every observation to a stable declared criterion ID, and publish every bound observation under exactly one criterion subsection in source order. Unsupported criteria receive one fixed structured gap, which prevents variable citation coverage or free-form gap labels across equivalent runs. Workers use canonical supplied-project-relative paths, scope findings to declared requirements, keep threshold sources separate from implementation evidence, and assign status strictly against each declared threshold. The replay scorer excludes Threshold Source lines from evidence and canonicalizes, de-duplicates, and sorts citations before grounding and stability signatures.
- Fragment selection now follows one closed selection contract across all eight evaluated TEA workflows. Each workflow starts from an empty set, evaluates complete stack, runner, package, and relevance gates, and loads only fragments named by matching rules. Deterministic checks reject every confirmed instruction ambiguity: tier-wide loading, flag-only Playwright Utils and Pact MCP gates, an unnamed Playwright Utils core set, nearby fragment references, and implicit contract relevance. They also hold every required oracle fragment to the workflow context that supplies it.
  The recorded Claude baseline remains unchanged with required recall 1, forbidden rate 0, and two unstable cases. Its investigation found a Python backend selecting `playwright-utils-mandate.md` against the existing oracle. A pre-change Codex substitution exposed the model-dependent form of the same ambiguity: all three framework cases selected different supersets across two repetitions, and the Python case selected `pactjs-utils-mandate.md` in both runs plus `pact-mcp.md` in one. The comparable Claude rerun was unavailable because that runner had reached its confirmed weekly usage limit; no further Claude calls were attempted. Bounded Codex reruns of the repaired framework, CI, and test-design contexts completed all 20 repetitions with required recall 1, forbidden rate 0, and zero unstable cases.
  Workflow-filtered fragment-selection runs now qualify diagnostic case identifiers with their workflow name. This closes the schema failure found while writing the pre-change Codex evidence and keeps focused JSON results tied to the manifest's declared case grid.
  Case-aware oracle validation rejects a required conditional fragment when the case config, runner, package, mobile stack, or contract-relevance facts do not open its positive load rule. Negative-only references cannot establish required ground truth.
- `bmad-tea` now asks one focused question when the supplied facts support several workflows. The routing guidance covers review versus trace, NFR evidence audit versus future test design, thin coverage across planning, automation, and measurement, and partially implemented scope across ATDD and automation. Direct requests to judge existing tests and recommend fixes route to Review Tests. Source-bound corpus validation, mutation checks, stored replays, and provenance-complete live evidence hold every ambiguity boundary and preserve clear and unservable routing behavior.
- The frozen schema 1.4.0 reader accepts later 1.4.0 diagnostics that emitted triage before the version advanced, while enforcing agreement with each diagnostic's failure class and root cause.
- ATDD red-phase generation now preserves supplied criterion ids, assigns stable collision-free ids to unnamed criteria, validates exactly one declared id per executable leaf, emits one transition-bearing red-phase leaf per criterion, defers secondary branches to green-phase automation, isolates each API criterion with a direct first assertion, keeps unimplemented setup responses opaque until that assertion, and gives E2E journeys a criterion-owned first failure boundary.

## [1.27.1] - 2026-09-17

### Added

- `test/lib/probe-targets.js`'s `probeCommandWithRetry` (TEA Story 5.2, stage two), a bounded retry over `probeCommand` for the two failure classes an ordinary network or CLI hiccup produces, `environment-timeout` and `environment-transport`. The retry set excludes `environment-configuration`, because the identical request has already been refused, and excludes `environment-parser` and `unexpected-error`, because another attempt cannot repair either condition. Found in the story's own first live run: one `tea-fragment-selection-runner` invocation hung past its declared budget and was killed, which lost the whole matrix even though the other hundreds of calls that run made all answered. At that volume, a per-call failure rate low enough to look rare still makes a fully clean run improbable, so Story 5.2's own AC1 ("every declared repetition completes") was gated on luck rather than reproducible.
  Scored calls complete on their first answered attempt. `probeCommand`'s `{ok: true}` means a real command answered; low scores and non-zero exits remain measurements. The bound permits three total attempts (`PROBE_RETRY_ATTEMPTS`), including the first. Every later attempt prints the interface, probe id, attempt number, and failure on `stderr`, so the transcript preserves the instability.
  All nine `test/eval-*.js` harnesses that call `probeCommand` for a live invocation, plus `test/lib/transcript-harness.js` (covering the `transcript` and `teach-me-testing` suites), now call `probeCommandWithRetry` instead; the two suites that never went through `probeCommand` at all, `automate` and `framework`, spawn Playwright and `npm install` directly and are unaffected. `test/test-probe-targets.js`'s `checkProbeRetry` proves every branch with fake ports: a retryable failure that recovers on the bound's last attempt reports the success and prints one retry line per failed attempt; a retryable failure that persists uses exactly the declared attempt bound and reports the final failure; a non-retryable class and a first-attempt success each run once and print nothing.
- The first complete live `eval:all` result is recorded for TEA Story 5.2 at repository commit `5c84a77f19576b075513dbbea9a74821cfe19f5d`, using `eval-quality` 3.4.0 and Claude Code 2.1.273 with the resolved `sonnet` model.
  All 109 declared repetitions across twelve suites completed in 9,830,418 ms with zero unaccounted skills and no environment failure.
  The run exited `1` with `failureClass: "quality"`: `automate`, `ci`, `test-review`, `transcript`, `framework`, and `teach-me-testing` met every threshold; `atdd`, `bmad-tea-routing`, `fragment-selection`, `nfr`, `test-design`, and `trace` recorded the measured quality findings in their suite results.
  `test/results/eval-all/latest.json` and `test/results/eval-all/history/2026-09-17T12-23-09-218Z.json` preserve the complete unedited run, including runner, model, versions, corpus and prompt digests, measurements, durations, and failure classes.

### Fixed

- Live probe retries for CI, NFR, test-design, and trace now create a fresh staged workspace and command port for every attempt, so an attempt that times out or loses transport cannot leave artifacts or progress checkpoints for a later successful attempt to score. Stored `eval:all` records also normalize runner executables to repository-relative paths or basenames, keeping the committed evidence portable without changing its measurements.
- Stable releases stream changelog notes to GitHub CLI through standard input, so a large release section cannot exceed the operating system's argument limit after npm publication.

## [1.27.0] - 2026-09-17

### Added

- Two stored results compare by `eval-quality`'s own dominance rule (TEA Story 5.1), rather than being eyeballed across two reports.
  `test/lib/compare-dominance.js`'s `compareStoredResults` wraps the package's `compareDominance`: it checks `comparabilityKey` first and refuses distinctly, with the reason, when two results do not measure the same scoring-policy-and-probe-set, rather than folding that case into the package's own `'incomparable'` as if it were a fifth ordinary verdict. Everything else, including each side's own `strength.comparable`, is left to the package's unmodified check; the wrapper adds one check ahead of the package's, it does not relax or duplicate what the package already does.
  `test/fixtures/dominance/comparable-results.json` is a hand-authored fixture, one pair per relation `DOMINANCE_RELATIONS` declares (`a-dominates-b`, `b-dominates-a`, `equivalent`, and `incomparable` built as a genuine tie — `a` wins the `defect` class, `b` wins `gameability`, so neither dominates, distinct from a `comparabilityKey` refusal that never reaches a relation at all), plus a fifth pair testing the refusal itself. `test/test-compare-dominance.js` holds every fixture's declared relation against the vocabulary through `publishedMember`, proves all four `DOMINANCE_RELATIONS` values are reachable, and proves the refusal and the `strength.comparable` honouring both fire on deliberately broken input before asserting the fixtures pass clean.
  AC3 names `test:eval-replay` as where this runs over stored data, but that script has no `EvidenceArtifact`-shaped data for any of its eight suites: their scorers are bespoke and never touch `eval-quality`'s `runScore`/`emit` pipeline, and reshaping eight scorers into `Outcome`/`Strength` output is a different story. The real analogue already in this repository is `test/test-probe-corpus.js`, which already replays every probe corpus against a stored baseline (`test/probes/expected-strength.json`) on every run: `probeSummary` now also records each probe's `comparableResult` (`comparabilityKey`, a slimmed `outcomes` array, and `strength`), and a new `dominanceProblems` check compares the stored baseline's `comparableResult` against the freshly measured one for every probe, reporting the named relation (e.g. `"dominance moved to \"b-dominates-a\""`) rather than only the existing literal-text diff `comparatorProblems` already caught. A probe where neither the stored nor the fresh side ever contributes a class (`hasContributingEvidence`) is skipped: comparing such a probe to itself would always read `'incomparable'`, which is a property of the probe class, not a finding. `dominanceComparatorProblems` proves the new check fires by running it against the corpus and itself (must report nothing) and then against one probe deliberately moved to a worse strength vector (must report exactly one change) — the same "seed one violation and watch it fail" discipline `comparatorProblems` already holds itself to, and confirmed live: mutating one committed probe's strength in `test/probes/expected-strength.json` and re-running reports `"dominance moved to \"b-dominates-a\" against the stored baseline"` beside the pre-existing line-diff, not instead of it.
  `test:compare-dominance` joins the `npm test` chain (right after `test:probe-corpus`) and `.github/workflows/quality.yaml`'s `validate` job in the same position.
- `bmad-testarch-automate`'s behavioral suite, closing the deferral `test/evals/suite-manifest.json` carried for it: `test/eval-automate.js` and four hand-authored Playwright spec sets under `test/fixtures/automate-eval/cases/` standing in for what a real run could produce against Story 6.6's `voucher-service` fixture. Unlike every other behavioral suite here, it builds no live-agent mode at all: every case's spec file runs for real, over HTTP, against the fixed implementation and against a scratch copy carrying the fixture's seeded minimum-spend regression, and `--validate-only` deliberately runs that same live cycle rather than skipping it, since there is no vendor call left for `--validate-only` to avoid spending. `--preflight-only` is the fast, static mode instead: it only checks the corpus and Playwright resolve, and starts nothing.
  `correct-run` asserts a redemption at a cart total exactly equal to a voucher's `minimumSpend`, the only input the seeded mutation changes the answer for; the scorer attributes the resulting mutated-run failure to the boundary through a declared message pattern. The other three cases each stand in for a different way generated coverage can look thorough and catch nothing: `misses-regression` asserts only interior values and passes on both runs, which the scorer reports as not detecting the regression rather than as a clean pass; `vacuous-pass` plants a status-code assertion no real response can fail; `duplicate` repeats one assertion under a second title. Vacuousness and duplication are confirmed statically, against each test's own source text, before either server starts.
  The suite's manifest entry declares `contracts: []`, which the schema allows for a suite with none yet, rather than an eval-quality contract: every contract this repository ships addresses a real command's real request shape, and `test/test-probe-targets.js` refuses one naming a CLI interface `test/lib/probe-targets.js`'s registry does not carry a real, shipped executable for, the same mistake that check exists to catch after eight contracts once named a fragment-selection runner that did not exist. This skill ships no live-agent command at all, so there is no real interaction for a contract to address, the same reason `atdd.contract.json`'s own oracles already stop short of `cli/atdd-red-check.js`'s deterministic half. `test/contracts/README.md` and the suite's own `$comment` record the reasoning.
  `eval:automate` and `test:eval-automate-data` (`--validate-only`) join `package.json`'s scripts, the latter in the `test` chain and in `.github/workflows/quality.yaml`'s `validate` job, right after the fixture's own `test:automate-eval-fixture` proof. `docs/explanation/eval-quality-roadmap.md`'s `bmad-testarch-automate` row narrows to the one residual outside this story's literal acceptance criteria, running the suite against a real model, the same way Story 6.5 narrowed `bmad-testarch-atdd`'s row.
- `test/eval-framework-scaffold.js`, `npm run eval:framework-scaffold`, and the `framework` suite in `test/evals/suite-manifest.json`, which install and smoke-test a generated `bmad-testarch-framework` scaffold for real. Story 6.8 scored the committed clean fixture's contents statically, with zero execution; nothing proved the scaffold actually installs or runs, which is exactly the gap this suite closes: it copies `test/fixtures/framework-scaffold/clean/` into a disposable workspace, runs a real `npm install`, and runs a real `npx playwright test tests/e2e/api-sample.spec.ts` against a minimal in-memory reservation stub (`test/lib/framework-scaffold-stub-server.js`), all inside NFR9 isolation. No vendor call is ever made -- the scaffold is a committed template copy, not live-agent output -- so `--agent` is accepted only for `eval:all`'s CLI uniformity and is never read; the manifest's `runnerCapabilities: ["command-execution"]` describes this harness's own sandboxed execution step, not a live agent's.
  `cli/lib/atdd-isolation.js`'s `selectBackend`/`sandboxedCommand`/`childEnvironment`/`probeBackend` are reused for both the sandboxed `npm install` and the sandboxed smoke test, every call passing the module's new `allowHostLoopback: true`. `test/lib/framework-scaffold-install-isolation.js` is the one new host-check surface this suite adds: a loopback-bound, CONNECT-only proxy that runs unsandboxed, on the host, with real egress of its own, and refuses any CONNECT target other than `registry.npmjs.org:443`. The sandboxed `npm install` is pointed at it through `HTTPS_PROXY`, so from inside the sandbox every registry request is a plain loopback connection, and the proxy is the one place deciding whether that loopback-forwarded request is allowed to leave the host at all.
  `allowHostLoopback` exists because this is the first caller of `cli/lib/atdd-isolation.js` that needs a host-side loopback service reachable at all, and bubblewrap's `--unshare-net` cannot serve it: verified live on CI (this story's own pull request going red on the runner after passing locally on macOS/seatbelt), bubblewrap's isolated network namespace has only its own loopback, never the host's, so a sandboxed `npm install` could not reach the proxy and a sandboxed smoke test could not reach the stub. seatbelt never had this limitation -- its profile denies network and re-allows loopback while the process still shares the host's real network stack -- so `allowHostLoopback` is a no-op there. On bubblewrap it drops `--unshare-net`, runs the whole sandboxed command as a fixed, unprivileged, non-root identity (`nobody`/`nogroup`) via `sudo setpriv`, and `ensureLoopbackOnlyEgress()` installs one `iptables` OWNER-match rule pair for that identity, once: loopback accepted, everything else rejected -- the kernel enforcing "loopback and nothing else" where a network-namespace primitive cannot express "allow one destination". `prepareWorkspaceOwnership`/`restoreWorkspaceOwnership` hand the workspace to that identity for the duration of each sandboxed call and back to the harness's own uid between calls, since a directory another uid owns cannot be written into or recursively deleted by anyone but its owner or root.
  A second, real defect surfaced only by testing this as a genuinely unprivileged sudoer rather than as root: `spawnSync`'s own `cwd` option runs the `chdir` as the _calling_ process's uid, before `sudo`/`setpriv` ever drops to the loopback-only identity, against a workspace `prepareWorkspaceOwnership` has already handed away -- `EACCES`, which Node reports as a failure to spawn `sudo` itself rather than naming the `chdir` that actually failed. Root never hits this (root can `chdir` into anything), which is why a first pass verified end to end as root, pushed clean, and still went red on CI: the GitHub Actions runner is an unprivileged sudoer, the one shape that was never actually tested. Fixed by moving the `chdir` into `bwrap`'s own `--chdir`, which runs after the identity switch, as the identity that already owns the directory; `sandboxedCommand`'s callers now pass `cwd` to `spawnSync` only for seatbelt, which never changes uid and has no `--chdir` of its own. Re-verified end to end, this time as an unprivileged user granted passwordless `sudo` the way the runner grants it, in the same `ubuntu:24.04` container: a direct attempt from the loopback-only identity to the real registry fails closed before any TLS handshake, a real `npm install` through the proxy, over loopback, succeeds end to end, and every other bubblewrap property this module already proved -- filesystem write scoping, the CPU budget, a spawned child's isolation matching its parent's -- holds identically under the new mode.
  `test/test-framework-scaffold-install-isolation.js` proves all of this before the suite is registered, house style of `test/test-atdd-isolation.js`: a non-allowlisted host is refused both directly from inside the sandbox and through the proxy, loopback still works, each proven directly and through a spawned child, plus a positive control (a second proxy instance, its allowlist target substituted to a loopback address) proving the allow path is a real decision and not the proxy refusing everything indiscriminately -- substituted specifically so the whole proof needs no external network and can run in the local `npm test` chain the same way the atdd isolation proofs do. Its own first draft had a real defect of the same class this story is about: the proxy-denial assertions matched a bare `/REFUSED/`, which the string `ECONNREFUSED` also satisfies, so on bubblewrap every one of them silently passed because the connection never reached the proxy at all, not because the proxy answered 403 -- caught by the same CI failure that surfaced the `--unshare-net` gap, fixed by printing and matching unambiguous tokens (`PROXY_REFUSED_403`/`PROXY_ALLOWED_200`) that share no substring with a connection-level error.
