  Two real defects surfaced by actually running the scaffold end to end, neither anticipated by the story that generated the fixture, both patched in the disposable workspace's own copy only -- `test/fixtures/framework-scaffold/clean/` (owned by Story 6.8/tea-47) is never touched: the fixture's `@seontechnologies/playwright-utils` pin (`^1.0.0`) does not resolve on the real npm registry, which only publishes 3.10.0 and above, so a real `npm install` against the committed pin fails with `ETARGET` before isolation or auth wiring is ever reached; and the scaffold's own `apiRequest` fixture (from `@seontechnologies/playwright-utils/api-request/fixtures`) destructures Playwright's built-in `page` fixture in its own signature, forcing a real, launchable Chromium even for `api-sample.spec.ts`, a pure JSON API test that calls no browser API at all -- the frozen spec's assumption that only `ui-sample.spec.ts` needs a browser does not hold for the scaffold as actually generated. `ensureChromiumBrowser()` provisions a Chromium headless shell into a harness-owned cache directory, unsandboxed and network-reaching by design -- run before the proxy or the sandboxed child exist for the run, the same placement this repository already gives bubblewrap and actionlint ahead of the suites that need them, though (grepped) no CI step calls this function yet, so today's first live run in any given job pays the download once -- and hands the sandboxed child `PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH` pointed at it, a real absolute path never derived from `$HOME` and therefore not hidden by the sandbox's HOME redirect. `ui-sample.spec.ts` stays out of scope; `cli/lib/atdd-net-guard.cjs` stays unmodified and is deliberately not loaded for these sandboxed children, since it refuses any `child_process` spawn from inside a Playwright worker unconditionally, which would refuse the one spawn (the browser launch) this suite depends on.
  `manageAuthToken` is patched in the disposable workspace's own copy of `auth-fixture.ts`, the one patch the frozen spec names directly: the committed fixture leaves it as a `throw`, `TODO: wire this up to the project's real auth endpoint`, since no real endpoint exists in this fixture. The patch returns a storage state naming the stub server's one fixed bearer token, built through the fixture's own already-declared `extractCookies` so the patch does not invent a second answer to what the auth cookie looks like, and persists it to disk via `saveStorageState`/`getStorageStatePath` -- a browser context's `storageState` option reads from that file path rather than from `manageAuthToken`'s return value directly, a requirement only found by actually running the scaffold (verified live: without the write, the smoke test failed with `ENOENT: .../.auth/local/default/storage-state.json`).
  A suite whose smoke test only ever runs against a working scaffold would not notice if its own assertions stopped asserting anything, so every live run seeds two independent, distinguishable breaks against the same installed workspace after the clean case has already passed -- no second `npm install`, since neither break changes what got installed. A second stub instance started with `createStatus` away from 201 must fail the smoke test's `expect(created.status).toBe(201)` with `Received: 500`; `breakAuthFixtureMerge` drops `authFixture` from the workspace copy's own `merged-fixtures.ts` `mergeTests(...)` call, in place, and must fail the same smoke test instead with Playwright's own `unknown parameter "authToken"`, since the spec destructures a fixture the call no longer merges in. Both are verified live, not asserted from theory, and either going undetected -- the smoke test passing anyway, or failing for some other reason -- is `seededDefectDetectionRate` (the manifest's second threshold, alongside `installAndSmokePassRate`) missing its target: a real quality finding about this suite's own sensitivity rather than about the scaffold.
  `npm run test:eval-framework-scaffold-data` (`--validate-only`: fixture files present, isolation backend selectable, no install, no network) joins the plain `npm test` chain, matching every sibling `test:eval-<suite>-data` convention; the full suite (`--agent claude`, real install, real network, both seeded defects) runs only through `eval:all`'s full-matrix tier. `.github/workflows/quality.yaml` gets two new steps in the `validate` job: the deterministic data check, and the install-time isolation proof (needs the same isolation backend as the atdd proofs, no external network). `tools/validate-ci-coverage.js` declares `eval:framework-scaffold` itself deliberately local: unlike `eval:automate`, it is not a free duplicate of a check already in CI, but a real `npm install` and a real Playwright/Chromium run that belongs in the manual/scheduled full-matrix tier alongside the live-agent evals.
  `test/evals/suite-manifest.json`'s `deferred` array drops its `bmad-testarch-framework` entry, the one this suite discharges, leaving `bmad-teach-me-testing` as the array's only remaining entry; its top `$comment`, which named `bmad-testarch-framework` as the one fragment-selection-spanned skill still deferred, now says none of the eight are. `docs/explanation/eval-quality-roadmap.md`'s "Coverage Still Owed" table gets a `framework` row reflecting the same discharge, and its intro sentence moves from two skills still needing a full behavioral eval to one (`bmad-teach-me-testing`); the "Everything above is measurement machinery" paragraph and its own skill count are corrected alongside it. `README.md`'s stated `npm test` chain length moves to sixty-seven with this story's two additions on top of the chain length recent prior stories already carried it to.
- `eval-quality-gates doc-invocations`, re-running the sixteen documented `npm run <script>` commands in `README.md`, `docs/explanation/eval-quality-adoption-guide.md`, and `test/README.md`'s CI-usage and individual-suites blocks against the real repository, so a renamed script or a broken example is caught by the build rather than by a reader. `eval-quality.config.json`'s new `doc-invocations` section declares each of the sixteen as a full literal spelling (`"npm run test:eval-data"`, never a shared `"npm run"` prefix), which is what keeps the gate from ever matching, let alone running, `npm run eval:all` or `npm run release:next` elsewhere on the same pages. `test:cli` carries a trailing comment on `test/README.md` too but is deliberately left undeclared: it is a separately sharded suite that takes 12-15 minutes, documented elsewhere (`.github/workflows/quality.yaml`'s own comments, and `package.json`) as intentionally apart from the bounded checks this gate covers. `doc-invocations.sampleInput` is set to `package.json`: the schema requires a file to stand in for a `<path>`-style metavariable, but none of the sixteen declared invocations across any of the three pages actually reach that substitution today, so it is inert and exists only so the section stays valid should a future declared line need one.
  `test/lib/doc-invocation-entry.js` is the section's `binary.entry`. The gate hands its entry only the text after the matched spelling, and a full per-script spelling means that text is the trailing `# ...` comment alone, so the entry is never told which of the eight scripts a given line names. It resolves that from the comment itself, through a hardcoded map of the eight exact comment strings to their script, and refuses (exit 64) before ever invoking real `npm` when a comment does not match one of them; a second, currently-unreachable path holds the same refusal for a token named directly ahead of a comment, in case a spelling is ever broadened to a shared prefix that would carry one. `timeoutMs` is set to 90000, twice the ~44s `test:probe-targets` takes measured locally, which is also why doc-invocations dispatches exactly the one script a line names rather than running all eight per invocation.
  Proved by two deliberate breaks, both restored after: renaming `test:eval-data` in `package.json` failed the gate at both pages it appears on, naming the file, line and npm's own "Missing script" error; separately, repointing the entry's own allowlist so `test:probe-targets`'s comment mapped to a script absent from `package.json` produced the same class of failure through the entry's dispatch instead. `npm run test:doc-invocations` reports a clean pass over 27 scanned invocations across the three pages today. It joins the `test` chain after `test:doc-counts` and runs in `.github/workflows/quality.yaml`'s `validate` job in the same position, keeping `test:ci-coverage` green.
  `test/test-doc-invocation-entry.js` holds the entry's comment-to-script lookup against every page's literal `npm run <script>  # <comment>` text, line by line, since `npm run test:doc-invocations` alone cannot catch two adjacent lines' comments being swapped: a swapped comment still resolves to some working script, so the mismatch between what a line spells out and what actually ran is invisible to the gate but not to this check. It also asserts `test:cli` stays undeclared even though its own page comments it the same way. It runs as `test:doc-invocation-entry`, chained right before `test:doc-invocations` the way `test:doc-count-sources` precedes `test:doc-counts`, with its own CI step. The entry itself now spawns `npm` through a shell on Windows (`npm` resolves to `npm.cmd` there), keeps exit 64 reserved for "no allowlisted script matches this line" by giving a real dispatch failure (an unspawnable `npm`, or a child killed by a signal) its own exit 1 instead, and names the signal when one is what stopped the child.
  `test/README.md` documents seven of the eight originally-declared scripts a second time, each with its own different trailing comment, plus eight more scripts in the same style (`test:schemas`, `test:install`, `test:knowledge`, `test:release-metadata`, `test:eval-nfr-data`, `test:eval-ci-data`, `test:eval-test-design-data`, `test:eval-routing-data`), all `--validate-only`/read-only/safe by the same bar as the original eight. Its comments were literal filenames (`test-agent-schema.js`, `tools/validate-eval-schemas.js`), and the gate's own faithfulness heuristic treats any token containing `/` or ending in a file extension as a path to realize against the real tree before the entry ever sees it -- confirmed by running the gate and watching several different scripts' comments arrive at the entry as the identical `sampleInput` placeholder path, an ambiguity no per-comment lookup could resolve. `test/README.md`'s fifteen relevant comments (all but `test:cli`'s) were trimmed to bare identifiers -- the `.js` extension dropped throughout, and the one `tools/` directory prefix dropped too, which also brings that one line in line with every sibling line's own bare-basename convention -- so none of them look like a path any more and the entry's lookup receives what the page actually says.
- `test/fixtures/automate-eval`, a fixed implementation and a qualified regression for Story 6.7's `bmad-testarch-automate` behavioral suite: `voucher-service`, a deterministic Node HTTP JSON voucher/discount redemption service mirroring `atdd-eval/reservations`'s shape (plain Node `http`, `PORT` env, JSON body, `playwright.config.ts`, `engines.node >= 22`), and one seeded regression, the minimum-spend boundary flipped from inclusive (`cartTotal >= voucher.minimumSpend`) to exclusive.
  The regression is the first `controlled-mutation` qualification in this repository backed by a live rollback. `tools/generate-probes.js`'s six existing `controlled-mutation` records assert `rollbackVerified: true` by comment: each is a permanent twin-file/twin-fixture-set pair with nothing to roll back. `test/test-automate-eval-fixture.js` applies the mutation to a scratch copy of `vouchers.js`, observes the boundary input wrongly rejected, reverts it, and observes it accepted again; only that live third pass earns `qualification.json`'s `rollbackVerified: true`, and removing the revert-and-recheck step fails the script (proved by removing it and watching the run fail before restoring it). `qualification.json` parses against `eval-quality`'s published `controlled-mutation` qualification shape, read off `probe.schema.json` since the package publishes no standalone validator for it; `targetArtifact` is digested at `vouchers.js`'s real, currently-committed bytes, a judgment this fixture's `README.md` states explicitly since no other fixture-level `controlled-mutation` record exists to confirm it against. A stale `targetArtifact` or evidence digest is collected and reported once the cycle has run, so a failing run still regenerates `evidence/*.json`.
  `test/test-automate-eval-fixture.js` also starts `server.js` as a child process and exercises `GET /health` and one accept case and one reject case of `POST /redeem`, giving the HTTP routing, body parsing, and error handling automated coverage the mutation cycle alone does not reach; `server.js` validates that a parsed body carries a numeric `cartTotal` and a string `redeemedOn` before calling `redeem()`, answering `400 { error: 'invalid-body' }` otherwise, and attaches an `'error'` listener on the catch-all 404 path so an aborted request there cannot crash the process.
  `npm run test:automate-eval-fixture` runs in the `test` chain and in `.github/workflows/quality.yaml`'s `validate` job. Builds no suite or manifest entry for `bmad-testarch-automate` itself; that is Story 6.7.
- The multi-turn transcript harness. TEA's eight existing suites each score exactly one agent invocation per case, so nothing could drive or record a multi-turn exchange, and `bmad-teach-me-testing` — a live, multi-turn teaching skill — has stayed permanently `deferred` for exactly that reason. `test/lib/transcript-harness.js`'s `runTranscript({ workspace, buildTurnPrompt, turnCount, runnerOptions })` is the reusable engine: N sequential calls against a new command, `tea-transcript-runner` (`cli/transcript-runner.js`, modeled on `cli/nfr-runner.js`), in one persistent workspace, so a turn's agent can read what an earlier turn left on disk even though each turn is a fresh process with no memory of its own. Every turn is one existing-shaped single-invocation call driven through `probeCommand`/`test/lib/probe-targets.js`, so authorization, output-capping, and budget enforcement stay identical to every other TEA runner call; what makes a session out of N such calls is entirely this new file. `buildTurnPrompt(turnIndex, priorTurns, workspace)` is the caller's own callback for shaping turn N from what turns before it produced, kept general on purpose: this story does not know what Story 6.11's real teaching session will ask it to do.
  `test/eval-transcript.js` registers a thin suite that proves the engine against a trivial, deterministic three-turn scripted session (`test/fixtures/transcript-eval/ground-truth.json`, `test/fixtures/transcript-runner/stub-agent.js`), and it never spends a real vendor call: `--agent custom --agent-cmd <stub>` is hardcoded regardless of the `--agent` flag `eval:all` was given, because this suite proves harness plumbing, not skill behavior. It carries no eval-quality contract, oracle, or probe-corpus machinery — plain assertions only, the shape `test/test-atdd-isolation.js` uses rather than the contract machinery `test/eval-nfr.js` carries — and `test/test-transcript-harness.js` is the proof: it drives the same stub's `contradictory` and `fail-turn-2` modes to show the cross-turn check actually fires and names the disagreement, and that a turn which exits nonzero stops the session rather than running the next turn blind, before `test/eval-transcript.js` is trusted to register anything.
  `test/schema/suite-manifest.js` gains a third `evalType`, `infrastructure`: a suite entry carrying it declares neither `skill` nor `skills`, so a harness-proof suite can register without implying coverage of a skill it does not measure — `bmad-teach-me-testing`'s `deferred` entry stays exactly as false-claim-free as it is today, until Story 6.11 actually measures it. `skillsOf` in `test/lib/suite-manifest.js`, and the record `suiteResultRecord` builds in `test/lib/eval-record.js`, both used to fall back to `[undefined]` for an entry with neither field; both now return `[]`, and `test/schema/eval-result.js`'s `skills` field (schema version 1.2.0) now allows empty for an `infrastructure` result while still requiring at least one skill for every other `evalType`, held by the schema's own `superRefine` rather than by `.min(1)` alone.
  `npm run eval:transcript`, `npm run test:transcript-harness`, and `npm run test:eval-transcript-data` are new; both `test:transcript-harness` and `test:eval-transcript-data` run in the `npm test` chain and in `.github/workflows/quality.yaml`'s `validate` job, next to the atdd isolation and scoring proofs and the other suites' own `test:eval-*-data` corpus checks they are shaped after.
- `test/test-framework-scaffold.js` and `npm run test:framework-scaffold`, which score a generated `bmad-testarch-framework` scaffold's contents. No generated scaffold was ever inspected before this: the workflow's step files describe the config, the fixtures, the sample tests, the package.json scripts, and a three-part hook registration, and nothing checked that a run actually produced what they describe. `test/fixtures/framework-scaffold/clean/` is the one canonical path this suite scores: frontend stack, Playwright, TypeScript, Playwright Utils enabled, no Pact. Ten categories score independently, each returning its own `{ ok, problems }` rather than one folded boolean: the Playwright config's timeouts and artifact settings, the environment files, the `merged-fixtures.ts` composition, the six-member `AuthProvider`, the two sample tests' import source, the `package.json` script, the docs, and the hook registration's three parts scored separately, `.claude/settings.json`'s `PreToolUse`/`PostToolUse`/`Stop` entries, `.tea/enforce-config.json`, and `.claude/hooks/tea-enforce.cjs` diffed byte for byte against the real source at `src/workflows/testarch/bmad-testarch-framework/resources/hooks/tea-enforce.cjs` rather than a second hand-maintained copy. It executes nothing: no agent call, no `npm install`, no network call.
  Every category is proven to fail. Eleven seeded mutations copy the clean fixture into a scratch temp directory, change one thing, rescore, and assert that exactly the targeted category fails while every other category still reports ok: a missing `actionTimeout`, a fixture dropped from the `mergeTests` call, a missing `AuthProvider` member, a sample test importing `@playwright/test` directly instead of the merged-fixtures entry point, a missing `test:e2e` script, an edited `tea-enforce.cjs`, `.tea/enforce-config.json` missing `hookSha256` and, separately, missing `testGlobs`, and `.claude/settings.json` missing only its `Stop` entry, named specifically, with `PreToolUse`/`PostToolUse` still reported present. Two more mutations go past the nine cases the story's own I/O matrix names: `.env.example` missing `BASE_URL` and `tests/README.md` missing its enforcement-hook documentation, since every category has to be provable to fail, not only the ones the matrix enumerates.
  `npm run test:framework-scaffold` runs in the `test` chain beside `test:enforce-hook` and in `.github/workflows/quality.yaml`'s `validate` job. Installing and smoke-testing the generated scaffold is Story 6.9's suite, declared separately so its wall clock and its network dependency never gate this one.
  `README.md`'s stated `npm test` chain length is held at fifty-seven with this and the three neighboring changes (`doc-invocations`, `automate-eval-fixture`, the transcript harness) all landed, and `npm run test:doc-counts` is what would have caught it going stale otherwise.
- The `teach-me-testing` behavioral suite, retiring `bmad-teach-me-testing` from the manifest's `deferred` array: the skill this whole epic has carried with no suite of any kind now has one, reusing Story 6.10's transcript engine and runner completely unchanged. One case, two turns, against a real vendor: turn 1 plays the whole first-session path through the real skill, itself supplying both the facilitator (following the real step files exactly) and a scripted learner (role, Beginner experience, learning goals, session 1, a deliberately wrong answer to session-1's own Q1, the resulting below-70% `[R]` review choice, and exit). Turn 2 is a brand-new, memoryless process in the same persistent workspace, which is the only thing that can tell a correct write of `progress.yaml` from a skill that would forget everything the instant turn 1's process exited, since a headless agent CLI carries no session state of its own between invocations.
  Every check is a mechanical containment or equality test over structural data, never a semantic judgment of reply quality: `placementHolds` asks whether `progress.yaml`'s `experience_level` equals the fixture's declared level; `correctionHolds` asks whether turn 1's transcript contains `step-04-session-01.md`'s own verbatim correction text for the seeded wrong answer; `reTeachingHolds` asks whether that same corrective content reappears in the transcript AFTER the learner's `[R]` choice, not merely once from the original mistake; `continuationHolds` asks whether turn 2 lands on `step-01-init.md`'s continuation greeting rather than its fresh-start one; and `masteryClaimHolds` cross-references every session `progress.yaml` claims `completed` against session-specific quiz evidence this fixture declares, rather than trusting the claim at face value — the cross-reference IS the honesty check, since the progress schema carries per-session `status`/`score` and no separate "mastery" field. `test/test-teach-me-testing-scoring.js` proves each of the four functions the story spec names actually fails on a hand-built violation (wrong placement, a missing correction, a review that never re-teaches the missed content, and a progress object claiming an untested session's mastery) and passes on a hand-built consistent case, no process spawn, before `test/eval-teach-me-testing.js` is trusted to score a real run with them.
  `progress.yaml` is read directly off the staged workspace with the file-system port after both turns complete, not through the probe's typed artifact channel, which `test/lib/transcript-harness.js` deliberately exposes no override for; each turn is also instructed to write its own complete transcript to a file in the workspace, read the same way, since a headless CLI's captured stdout is the only channel `runTranscript` records and a written file survives even if a final printed reply were ever truncated.
  **AC2 was rewritten before this suite was built.** The original AC2 described a fixture-declared remediation topic adapted to after a wrong answer; all seven `steps-c/step-04-session-0N.md` files and `data/curriculum.yaml` were read in full and no such mechanism exists. `bmad-teach-me-testing` has no mechanism, in any session, where a wrong answer on one topic pulls in a different topic the pre-correction plan did not already have — the real branch is `[R]` review the same session's content again, or `[C]` continue anyway, and `reTeachingHolds` scores that real branch instead. The finding and the rewritten criterion are recorded in `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/epics.md` itself; misconception-triggered topic routing is a product question about the skill, out of this story's scope.
  **No eval-quality contract for v1**, the same declared deviation `test/eval-transcript.js` records for its own reason: every check here is the same containment/equality shape `test/eval-atdd.js`'s `scoreRun` already uses for comparably structural claims, and `test/test-teach-me-testing-scoring.js`'s own pure-function proof is what gives that shape independent verification without a contract adding value the proof does not already give. Every other real behavioral suite in this repository carries one; this is a deliberate, reviewable deviation for review to weigh, not an oversight.
  `test/lib/probe-targets.js`'s `tea-transcript-runner` target backstop moves from 11 to 21 minutes, one minute above this suite's own 20-minute per-turn `RUN_TIMEOUT_MS`: a real teaching turn reads several step files, plays both facilitator and learner through an entire session, and writes a progress file, session notes, and a transcript log, materially heavier than the trivial scripted exchange Story 6.10 proved the engine against, and `commandTargetPolicy`'s budget override can only lower this ceiling, never raise it.
  `npm run eval:teach-me-testing`, `npm run test:teach-me-testing-scoring`, and `npm run test:eval-teach-me-testing-data` are new; both `test:teach-me-testing-scoring` and `test:eval-teach-me-testing-data` run in the `npm test` chain and in `.github/workflows/quality.yaml`'s `validate` job, next to the transcript engine's own proof and corpus check. `test/evals/suite-manifest.json`'s top comment also corrects a stale count found while adding this entry: it read "three of the eight" fragment-selection-spanned skills as deferred and listed the other five's suites by name, omitting `atdd`; the real count was always two (`bmad-testarch-automate`, `bmad-testarch-framework`), with six covered, `atdd` included.
- `eval-quality-gates doc-counts`, holding hand-written counts against the source that computes them: the roadmap's per-suite `eval:all` call counts (`docs/explanation/eval-quality-roadmap.md:30`), the 59/24/19/16 knowledge-fragment tier breakdown (`README.md:215`), the npm-test-chain length (`README.md:395`), the fragment-selection case count (`README.md:400`), and the same per-suite call counts restated two more times, once in `docs/explanation/eval-quality-adoption-guide.md:322` and once in `README.md:424` (which also states the three-runner total). `test/lib/doc-count-sources.js` computes every value: the seven per-suite call counts are `suite-manifest.json`'s `caseCount` times its `repetitions`, except `test-review`, whose harness calls `runReview()` once per repetition over the whole three-file corpus with no per-case loop, so its count is `repetitions` alone. The manifest is read through `test/lib/suite-manifest.js`'s existing `suiteById` and validated by its schema first, the same discipline every other manifest consumer already carries. The chain length reuses `tools/validate-ci-coverage.js`'s own `chainedScripts`, so the printed count and the held sentence can never disagree.
  The call counts are sourced from `repetitions`, not from a harness's own internal default: `test/eval-all.js`'s `repetitionsFor()` always passes `--runs <manifest repetitions>` to the child harness unless overridden on the command line, overriding whatever default the harness falls back to standalone. A first pass at this sourced the counts from a `DEFAULT_RUNS` constant added to each harness instead, which is not what a real `eval:all` run spends; retuning a suite's `repetitions` in the manifest left that version of the gate reporting zero disagreements while the real call count moved. Proved by setting `fragment-selection`'s `repetitions` to 3 in the manifest: all three held sentences now fail, each naming its own reading against the real 72 (up from 48), and the two totals against 123 and 369.
  Two more stale counts turned up while building the inventory and are corrected and now held in the same PR: `README.md:395`'s "`npm test` chains thirty-three checks" (now forty, and the sentence's second restatement of the same number is held too, not only the first) and `docs/explanation/eval-quality-adoption-guide.md:322`'s "One `npm run eval:all` for one runner spends 95 calls," which omitted `test-design` from its own itemized list entirely; the correct total, and the one now held, is 99.
  `doc-count-sources.js` also holds every other `src/workflows/testarch/*` workflow's own copy of `tea-index.csv` against the one it reads the tier breakdown from: the two copies have to carry the exact same set of fragment ids, each tagged with the exact same tier, and a workflow that ships a `resources/knowledge` directory but no `tea-index.csv` beside it fails rather than being skipped (a first pass filtered out a missing copy with `fs.existsSync` and only checked ids the sibling itself carried, so a deleted or truncated copy passed; a review round caught it, both gaps are closed, and `bmad-teach-me-testing`, which ships no knowledge directory at all, is excluded the same way `test/test-knowledge-base.js` already excludes it, by the directory's absence rather than by name). `test/test-doc-count-sources.js` recomputes the seven per-suite call counts, both totals, and the fragment-tier breakdown independently of `doc-count-sources.js`; proves the tier-sum and cross-copy guards fire (a corrupted tier, a missing sibling copy, a missing row, and an extra row, each against a scratch copy or a real sibling restored in a `finally`, never left corrupted); and proves each `doc-counts` entry's `counts` array names its sources in the order its pattern's capture groups actually carry them, since `TEST_DESIGN_CALLS`, `NFR_CALLS` and `TRACE_CALLS` are all `4` today and a transposed pair would otherwise report zero disagreements forever. `npm run test:doc-count-sources` and `npm run test:doc-counts` both run in the `test` chain and in `.github/workflows/quality.yaml`'s `validate` job, next to `test:criteria-fragments`.
  Rebasing onto #186 (the `ci` eval suite) exposed the same class of drift this gate exists to catch, before this PR even merged: `README.md:424` had already been rewritten by #186 to add a `ci`/"pipelines" clause and the correspondingly higher totals (103, 309), but `docs/explanation/eval-quality-roadmap.md:30` and `docs/explanation/eval-quality-adoption-guide.md:324` had not, leaving two sentences describing the same `eval:all` invocation disagree about how many suites it runs. `doc-count-sources.js` now exports `CI_CALLS` (`ci`'s `caseCount` times its `repetitions`, the same formula as the other five per-case suites) and folds it into `TOTAL_CALLS`; all three sentences now name the `ci` suite and hold the same seven-suite total, proven the same way as every other entry here, by reverting each figure and watching the gate name it.
- `test/eval-nfr.js` scores whether a domain's citations speak to the status they were cited for, not only whether each resolves to a real file. `parseReport` grew a `currentCriterion` pointer alongside `currentDomain`, so `entry.citations` is now also tracked per criterion in `entry.criteria`; a `###` heading exactly one level under a domain's own opens one and Resource Usage's two un-headed bold bullets (`**CPU Usage**`, `**Memory Usage**`) promote to their own, matched against `ground-truth.json`'s `criteria[].name` after stripping every trailing `(...)` annotation from both sides, since a live model decorates a heading (`MTTR (Mean Time To Recovery)`, `Compliance (GDPR)`) far more freely than the two hand-built fixture reports alone had shown. `scoreRun` reads that map: over the criteria ground truth declares evidence for, whose citation is real (not already counted by `maxFabricatedEvidence`) and present at all, a criterion is grounded when every one of its real citations, not just one of them, names a file ground truth declares for it; `groundedCitationAccuracy` is that share and `ungroundedCitations` names only the citations that failed to match. A criterion with no declared evidence, chiefly the gapped bundle's Test Coverage and Code Duplication, is excluded rather than scored, since citing the tech spec there to state the threshold and record that no report exists is correct, not a miss.

  AC2 forbade declaring the threshold from the stored corpus alone. Two designs were worked out by hand first: exempt evidence-free criteria (a clean 1.0 bar against a ≈94% known-bad case), or count every criterion uniformly (an intrinsic ≈94.7% miss rate even on a correct run, against ≈84% for the known-bad case, an uncomfortably tight 0.921–0.947 band). `node test/eval-nfr.js --agent claude --runs 2` against both bundles settled it: the exempting design scored 72/72 (100%) across all four live repetitions, with every real evidence citation the model made landing on the right criterion; the uniform design's supposed noise band turned out to be a fixed 72/76 (94.7%) every single repetition, a constant baked into the bundle rather than something live model behavior varies. `groundedCitationAccuracy`'s ceiling is 1, calibrated against `--agent claude` only; `codex` and `gemini` are not sampled and `test/README.md` now says so.

  Every other threshold is unaffected: `parseReport`'s existing extraction paths, and every other check `scoreRun` already made, are unchanged, so a score before and after this change is comparable on `domainStatusAccuracy`, `undecidableDomainAccuracy`, `thresholdFidelityAccuracy`, `overallStatusAccuracy`, `domainCoverage`, and every ceiling. `test/replay/nfr/gapped-fault-tolerance-grounded-on-spec/` is the one stored case AC1 names, and the one that moves: it now scores 16 of 17 grounded, Fault Tolerance's citation of `docs/tech-spec.md` where ground truth wants `evidence/reliability-2026-08.json`. `signatureOf` and `projectNfrResult` both grew `groundedCriteria` and `ungroundedCitations`, since two runs can carry the identical flat citation list while disagreeing on which criterion each file was cited for. The nfr corpus's own stored `scorerVersion` stamps move from 9 to 10, the atdd suite's own already-merged number (see its own entry, below, for what moved it), which this work did not move but does now share; every stored nfr result that scores at all grew the two new fields, seventeen reproduce a clean 1.0 and the fault-tolerance case is the only one whose value moved. No `test-review`, `test-design`, `trace` or `routing` case moved.

  Four review passes (two subagent layers plus one peer session, run before this PR opened) found two real gaps the corpus above did not exercise. First, grounding read `real.some(...)`: a criterion cited with the right file and one extra real-but-wrong one scored fully grounded, and the wrong file never reached `ungroundedCitations`, narrower than AC1's own wording, which names no exception for a wrong citation riding alongside a correct one. Second, `parseReport` promoted any heading nested under a domain to the next criterion whatever its depth, so a model writing an unplanned sub-heading between a criterion's own heading and its Evidence line (`#### Root Cause Note` under `### MTTR`) silently reassigned `currentCriterion`, and that criterion's later citations vanished from both the numerator and the denominator of `groundedCitationAccuracy` with nothing printed anywhere. Both are fixed: grounding now requires every real citation to match (`real.every(...)`), and a nested heading is promoted only when it sits exactly one level under the domain, the depth `nfr-report-template.md` actually gives a criterion, so a model's own aside nested deeper cannot steal a still-open criterion's citations. Two new stored cases prove each one both ways, reverting the fix and replaying: `test/replay/nfr/gapped-fault-tolerance-extra-wrong-citation/` (a second, wrong citation added to `gapped-correct-audit`'s already-correct Fault Tolerance entry) and `test/replay/nfr/gapped-mttr-stray-subheading/` (a `#### Root Cause Note` inserted between MTTR's own lines). Neither stored case in the pre-existing corpus moved.

  Three lower-severity findings from the same passes are closed too. `stripCriterionAnnotation` strips every trailing `(...)` group rather than only the last one, since a heading chaining two (`MTTR (Mean Time To Recovery) (revised)`) defeated a single strip. `CRITERION_BULLET_ALIASES` is looked up case-insensitively with a trailing colon stripped, since `**CPU Usage:**` and `**cpu usage**` name the bullet a live model could as easily write. `validateCorpus` gained two checks: two declared criteria in one domain stripping to the same name, which would silently share one citation list in `scoreRun`, and `CRITERION_BULLET_ALIASES`'s two target strings no longer naming a criterion `ground-truth.json` declares; `test/test-nfr-validate-corpus.js` seeds each defect onto a cloned copy of the real corpus and asserts the check fires, rather than only asserting the real corpus stays clean. A third new stored case, `test/replay/nfr/gapped-decorated-criterion-headings/`, exercises the three live-observed decorated headings the stored corpus never had (`MTTR (Mean Time To Recovery)`, `Compliance (GDPR)`, `Disaster Recovery (evaluated separately)`) and, for the first time outside a fenced block, Disaster Recovery's own un-headed RTO/RPO bullet shape; reverting `stripCriterionAnnotation` to a no-op and replaying it drops `groundedCriteria` from 17/17 to 13/13.

  A fourth pass, run after the fixes above landed, found three more real gaps, each independently surfaced by the peer session and at least one subagent layer. `scoreRun` rebuilt `byStrippedName` with `new Map(reportedCriteria.map(...))`, keyed on stripped name; two report headings stripping to the same name collapsed into one entry and the later `.set()` silently discarded the earlier heading's citations, so a wrong citation under the first of two same-stripped headings was never examined once a second, correctly-cited heading came after it. `byStrippedName` now concatenates every heading's citations under a shared key instead. Second, the `EVIDENCE_GAPS_HEADING` branch set `inGaps` without clearing `currentCriterion`, so a per-domain `### Evidence Gaps` subsection (the template's own shape, one level under a domain, distinct from the report's single top-level `## Evidence Gaps` section) left whatever criterion was still open; every domain-section line feeds `citationsIn` regardless of label, so a `**Suggested Evidence:**` line naming a gap's missing evidence was read as grounding for that still-open criterion instead. `EVIDENCE_GAPS_HEADING` now clears `currentCriterion` the same way a domain heading does. Third, `stripCriterionAnnotation`'s own single trailing-group regex could not describe two parenthetical shapes it was written to handle: a chained decoration (`MTTR (Mean Time To Recovery) (revised)`) left the first group unstripped, and a nested one (`Disaster Recovery (evaluated separately (see appendix))`) defeated the regex's `[^()]*` capture entirely, leaving the whole annotation untouched. It is now an iterative balanced-paren peel, closing over the same balanced-nesting class `stripFencedCodeBlocks` already handles for fenced code. Three new stored cases prove each fix both ways: `test/replay/nfr/gapped-compliance-duplicate-heading-hides-wrong-citation/` (Compliance's heading split in two, the first carrying a wrong citation the second's correct one used to hide; reverting the concatenation drops `groundedCriteria` from 16/17 to 17/17 and empties `ungroundedCitations`), `test/replay/nfr/gapped-nested-evidence-gaps-leaks-citation/` (a `### Evidence Gaps` subsection inserted under Reliability, after Disaster Recovery; reverting the clear drops `groundedCriteria` from 17/17 to 16/17), and `test/replay/nfr/gapped-chained-and-nested-parenthetical-headings/` (MTTR chained, Disaster Recovery nested; reverting the peel drops `groundedCriteria` from 17/17 to 15/15). No case in the pre-existing corpus moved and `SCORER_VERSION` stays 10: none of the three fixes changes what any already-stored report scores, only what a report shaped like the three new cases would have scored, so no published artifact disagrees with the new reading.

- `eval-quality-gates doc-claims`, holding TEA's published prose claims against the artifact each one describes, across `symbols`, `lists`, `codes`, `fences`, and `dated`. `citations`, `vocabulary`, and `transcriptions` are deliberately unadopted for now, recorded as such in `eval-quality.config.json`'s `doc-claims-omitted-classes` key rather than silently absent: `citations` because every `path:line` style reference found in the scanned pages names a fixture in a hypothetical user project a walkthrough describes rather than a line in TEA's own source; `vocabulary` because every accept/refuse candidate found needs either a custom verb list or reworded prose to produce a sentence the gate's verb-governance heuristic can actually classify, and configuring one on a guess is the false-green this gate exists to close; and `transcriptions` because every candidate found is either a bare number (`doc-counts` territory, adopted above) or a fenced bash-invocation block (`doc-invocations` territory, Story 4.3), not a byte-for-byte literal string block TEA's own code emits.
  `test/lib/doc-claim-sources.js` derives every value from the artifact that owns it: the criteria-registry's mobile and playwright-utils row ids are read from `criteria-registry.md`'s own table via the parser `tools/validate-criteria-fragments.js` already exports, rather than a second copy of its cell-count and id-shape rules; the review-verdict and skip-payload JSON schemas are built from `VERDICT_KEYS`/`SKIP_KEYS` themselves, so a key added or dropped there changes what the docs' worked examples are held against with no second edit; `FUTURE_KEYS` is read from `module.yaml`'s own "⏭️ FUTURE" marker positions rather than hand-listed, so a different key gaining or losing a marker is caught even when the marker count stays the same; and `keyIsUnread` checks a key's word boundary generally, not only the `{key}` interpolation shape, since a "wired" variable is as often read as a bare backtick name or a `config.` prefix in a checklist as it is interpolated into a template.
  `dated.triggers` covers the claim shapes the story asks for generally (`not yet`, `currently`, `as of`, `today`) rather than one bespoke phrase per known sentence, which is what a "new unregistered claim of that shape fails" guarantee actually requires; the broader scan surfaced one already-false claim (`eval-quality-roadmap.md`'s "the pin is 3.0.0 now", corrected to 3.2.0) and nine more genuine dated claims now registered, eight of them held by human review with a stated reason rather than a predicate, since nothing in the tree can decide a subjective depth-parity judgment or a claim about history outside the repository. `codes.pattern` similarly widened from a backtick-anchored `` exits `N` `` form to also catch the bare `exit N`/`Exit N` forms scattered through the docs, since the first pattern missed roughly three-quarters of the real exit-code mentions in the corpus it was meant to hold.
  Building the `VERDICT_SCHEMA`/`SKIP_SCHEMA` pair found two real drift bugs in `docs/reference/tea-test-review-cli.md`'s own worked examples: the verdict JSON was missing `advisoryObservations`, a required key `cli/lib/parse-report.js` has extracted since it added Advisory Observations to the Executive Summary parser, and the skip JSON was missing the required `gateOn` key entirely. Both examples are corrected, and the prose describing `advisoryObservations` is added beside `keyStrengths`/`keyWeaknesses`. Two functions this gate needed to reach, `RECOMMENDATION_ENUM` and `EXIT`, are now exported from `cli/lib/parse-report.js` and `cli/test-review.js`; neither previously left its module.
  A second finding surfaced while proving the `lists` entries: a first draft pointed a list's `tokenShape` at the same module as its `expected` set, which made the check tautological — removing a member from the source removed it from both the "what counts as a member" filter and the "what's expected" set at once, so the two shrank together and the gate stayed green. `tokenShape` now uses a shape pattern (a capitalized phrase, or a registry row id) instead, decoupled from the current membership of the set it's held against. `symbols.foreign` also had two real misclassifications, `authToken` (a playwright-utils fixture parameter, not an environment variable) and `VERSION` (an `eval-quality` export, not a platform name), both corrected. Every configured entry was proven by reverting the artifact it describes and watching the gate fail, then restoring it and watching the gate pass. `npm run test:doc-claims` runs in the `test` chain and in `.github/workflows/quality.yaml`'s `validate` job, next to `test:criteria-fragments`.
  `test/test-doc-claim-sources.js` proves the module's own derivation logic independently: `FUTURE_KEYS` against a second, independent walk of `module.yaml`'s markers, `VERDICT_SCHEMA`/`SKIP_SCHEMA` against every required key in turn, `keyIsUnread`'s word-boundary behavior against a staged probe file, and the CONCERNS count against an independent read of `expected-strength.json`. `npm run test:doc-claim-sources` runs in the `test` chain and in `.github/workflows/quality.yaml`'s `validate` job, next to `test:doc-claims`.
  An adversarial review round against this section recorded ten findings; each has a disposition. Fixed: `eval-quality-roadmap.md:170`'s one dated sentence carrying three separate dated assertions is split into three lines, since the gate holds one registered claim per physical line — "as of `eval-quality` 1.2.0" and "since 3.0.0" are now registered too, closing the previously-unreferenced `EVAL_QUALITY_AT_LEAST_3_0_0` export (findings 1, 2); five `dated.triggers` entries that were strict substrings of four broader ones already in the array are dropped (finding 3); `FUTURE_KEYS`'s group-boundary scan now recognizes hyphenated top-level keys (`module.yaml`'s own `post-install-notes:`) instead of tunnelling through everything under it on the incidental luck that nothing there happened to match a narrower regex (findings 4, 5); `keyIsUnread`'s `src/workflows` walk and file reads are memoized at module scope instead of repeating on each of its up to eight calls per module load (finding 9); the `citations`-omission reason is corrected to say what is actually true — eight `path:line`-shaped strings exist in the scanned pages, none resolving against TEA's own source, rather than zero existing at all (finding 8); the `tea-test-review-cli.md:289` "capped at ten items" claim now has a `doc-counts` entry against a new `ADVISORY_OBSERVATIONS_MAX_ITEMS` export, promoted out of `extractAdvisoryObservations`'s bare default parameter so the two cannot drift independently (finding 7, landed once the rebase below brought Story 4.1's `doc-counts` section in); and the `MOBILE_ROW_IDS`/`PLAYWRIGHT_UTILS_ROW_IDS` test now parses `criteria-registry.md` independently instead of re-calling the shared parser with the same predicate strings production already uses (finding 10). Skipped: the `commands.md:272` `deriveRecommendation` restatement stays unregistered, since it genuinely fits none of the five adopted classes and forcing it into one would be the tautological false-green this story's own brief warns against (finding 6).
  Rebasing onto Story 4.1's `doc-counts` section (#188) and the `ci` eval suite (#186) it had itself rebased onto surfaced real drift in five already-registered `doc-claims` entries, all fixed here: `bmad-testarch-ci` leaving the manifest's `deferred` array moved "five skills deferred" to four (`eval-quality-roadmap.md`) and "four of the eight fragment-selection skills deferred" to three, with `ci` joining the five now discharged by their own suites (`eval-quality-adoption-guide.md`); the new `ci` probe corpus moved the stored CONCERNS count from 33 to 34; and the command adapter's own "N of the 59 cannot pre-flight today" sentence, already correctly reworded by #186 from its "55" wording, needed its `read`-settled `dated.claims` key moved to match. Two more stale counts the same rebase exposed and this PR corrects along the way, neither one a `dated` claim: the roadmap's "55 probes across fourteen corpora" (now 59 across fifteen) and the adoption guide's "`strictPromotable` for all 55 scored probes" (now 59). `README.md`'s deterministic-checks section gains a `test:doc-claims` bullet alongside `test:doc-counts`, so the "Six of the forty-one" sentence's own `doc-counts` entry (Story 4.1's) is retargeted to "Seven of the forty-three."
  A second rebase, onto the `atdd` behavioral suite (#187) which merged mid-review, moved the same manifest-derived numbers again before this PR's own CI even finished: `bmad-testarch-atdd` leaving `deferred` too dropped the deferred count from four to three (`FOUR_SKILLS_DEFERRED`/`THREE_FRAGMENT_SELECTION_SKILLS_DEFERRED` renamed `THREE_SKILLS_DEFERRED`/`TWO_FRAGMENT_SELECTION_SKILLS_DEFERRED`, both re-derived and re-proved the same way), the roadmap's "remaining four skills"/"measures six skills" moved to "three"/"seven", the adoption guide's "three of the eight ... still deferred" moved to "two", and the roadmap's separate "Four of TEA's ten skills still need a full behavioral eval" (untracked by any gate, found while re-checking the surrounding numbers) is now "Three." The npm-test-chain length moved again too, from forty-three to forty-seven (atdd's own four new gates: `test:atdd-isolation`, `test:atdd-net-guard`, `test:atdd-scoring`, `test:eval-atdd-data`), so `README.md`'s "Seven of the forty-three" is now "Seven of the forty-seven," re-proven against the real merged `package.json` chain via `tools/validate-ci-coverage.js`'s own `chainedScripts` rather than a hand count.
  A third rebase, onto #191 ("hold layering, package boundary and field lineage") which also merged mid-review, bumped the `eval-quality` pin from 3.2.0 to 3.3.0 and added the `dependency-direction`, `package-boundary` and `field-ownership` gates to the same `eval-quality.config.json` this story's `doc-claims` section lives in — a real three-way content merge, not a mechanical resolution. The pin move falsified `EVAL_QUALITY_PIN_IS_3_2_0` (renamed `EVAL_QUALITY_PIN_IS_3_3_0`) and the roadmap's own "The pin is 3.2.0 now" sentence, both corrected and re-proved by bumping the pin past 3.3.0 and watching the claim fail naming itself. `#191` also turned `dependency-direction` from report-only to failing and flagged two genuine violations in this story's own new files: `test/lib/doc-claim-sources.js` and `test/test-doc-claim-sources.js` each read `package.json` via a relative `require()`, which escapes their declared `test/` root; both now read it as data through `fs.readFileSync`, the same fix #191 already applied elsewhere in the repository for the identical class of violation. `package-boundary`, also newly enforced, flagged this story's own `ADVISORY_OBSERVATIONS_MAX_ITEMS` docblock in the shipped `cli/lib/parse-report.js` for citing `docs/reference/tea-test-review-cli.md` as a relative path the published tarball does not carry; reworded to name the file without the unshippable path prefix. `README.md`'s chain-length sentence moves once more, "Seven of the forty-seven" to "Seven of the fifty-three," matching #191's own six new chain steps.
  A fourth rebase, onto #194 ("execute the fenced commands in the documentation," Story 4.3) which merged while the third rebase's CI was still settling, added a `doc-invocations` section to `eval-quality.config.json` alongside this story's `doc-claims` and inserted `test:doc-invocation-entry`/`test:doc-invocations` into `package.json`'s test chain at the same point this story's own `test:doc-claim-sources`/`test:doc-claims` already occupy, both real content merges rather than mechanical resolutions. `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/epics.md` needed the same two-line proximity merge as the chain string, since Story 4.2's and Story 4.3's tracker lines sit adjacent and both stories had annotated their own line the same round. No `doc-claims`/`doc-counts` claim moved this round; the npm test chain grew from fifty-three to fifty-five (`doc-invocations`' own two new gates), so `README.md`'s "Seven of the fifty-three" is now "Seven of the fifty-five," re-proven against `chainedScripts()` rather than a hand count. `npm run test:doc-invocations` also incidentally re-proves the port-collision flake #194 fixed in `test/eval-atdd.js` (a fixed fixture-server port colliding under concurrent `test:probe-targets` runs) stays fixed, since the gate runs that exact script as one of its documented invocations.
  A fifth rebase, onto #193 ("build the automate-eval fixture's fixed implementation and qualified regression," Story 6.6), which merged while the fourth rebase's CI was still finishing, added one `test:automate-eval-fixture` step to the same `package.json` chain string this story's own `test:doc-claim-sources`/`test:doc-claims`/`test:doc-invocation-entry`/`test:doc-invocations` already occupy — a proximity conflict on the same JSON string value rather than a substantive overlap, since #193 touches neither `eval-quality.config.json` nor any file this story's `doc-claims` entries hold a claim against. No claim moved. The npm test chain grew from fifty-five to fifty-six; `README.md`'s "Seven of the fifty-five" is now "Seven of the fifty-six," re-proven against `chainedScripts()`.
  A sixth rebase, onto #195 ("build the multi-turn transcript harness," Story 6.11's infrastructure), which merged before the fifth rebase's push had even landed, added `test:transcript-harness` and `test:eval-transcript-data` to the same `package.json` chain string, again a proximity conflict rather than a substantive one: the new `transcript` suite is `evalType: infrastructure`, declares no skill, and adds nothing to `suite-manifest.json`'s `deferred` array or to any probe corpus, so none of this story's manifest-derived claims moved. No `eval-quality.config.json` overlap either. The npm test chain grew from fifty-six to fifty-eight; `README.md`'s "Seven of the fifty-six" is now "Seven of the fifty-eight," re-proven against `chainedScripts()`.
  The AC3 gap noted above is closed: `eval-quality` 3.4.0 (pin bumped from 3.3.0) ships the `dated.claims[].asOf` staleness pin — a normalized-content sha256 of an evidence file, taken the moment a human confirmed a `settles: "read"` claim true against it, that fails the gate the moment that file's content changes. Of the ten `read`-settled claims this section carries, only one gets a pin: `eval-quality-adoption-guide.md`'s "TEA's state today, as `test/evals/suite-manifest.json` registers it:" is a table hand-transcribed from that file's own suites and deferred entries, a real evidence file whose content genuinely determines the claim, so `asOf.subject` names it explicitly (the table's own page would protect nothing new). The other nine stay bare on the same test applied case by case: two ("have never been run live," on `README.md` and the adoption guide) are facts about history outside this repository; `verification-architecture.md`'s four "today"/"not yet equivalent"/"reasoning transfers" claims are holistic, cross-cutting judgments the page itself already says derive from no single artifact, and pinning any of them would default `asOf.subject` back to the same page carrying the sentence, which the gate's own docs call worse than no pin at all; the roadmap's "TEA currently has three layers of self-validation" is the same shape, an editorial grouping with "no single source" behind it; and the command adapter's "of the 59 cannot pre-flight today" is a deliberately historical snapshot ("a historical measurement, not a live-rechecked invariant," a decision already recorded in its own `reason` before this pass) that pinning would silently re-litigate into a live-tracked one. The hash is generated with `eval-quality`'s own shipped `hashOfSubject` (imported directly from `node_modules/eval-quality/dist/gates/check-doc-claims.js`, since it isn't re-exported through the package's public subpath map), never hand-rolled, because the normalization is depth-canonical outside fenced code blocks and byte-exact inside them. Proved by editing `test/evals/suite-manifest.json`'s own `$comment` text and watching `test:doc-claims` fail naming both the stored and the freshly computed hash, then restoring it and watching the gate pass again. Bumping the pin itself required no other change: the full `npm test` chain was already green against 3.4.0 before any `asOf` entry existed.
  `tools/regenerate-doc-claim-hash.mjs` (`npm run regenerate:doc-claim-hash -- <subject-path>`) closes the follow-on gap: `hashOfSubject` is not part of `eval-quality`'s published API — `dist/gates/check-doc-claims.js` exports it, but that path sits outside every declared subpath, and `eval-quality-gates --help` carries no regeneration subcommand of its own — so re-confirming a pin after its `subject` file legitimately changes had no documented or scripted path. The script is `.mjs` rather than `.js`, matching `eval-quality`'s own `dist/gates/audit-lockfile-age.mjs`/`check-doc-invocations.mjs`/`check-licenses.mjs`: `dependency-direction`'s `tools` root only declares the `.js` extension, and a relative `require()` reaching into `node_modules/` would fail its "escapes the declared scan roots" rule regardless of extension, since that rule has no exemption for a relative specifier (only a bare one's static `import` can be exempted, per `check-dependency-direction.js`'s own `ImportExemption`). It intentionally only prints the hash rather than rewriting `eval-quality.config.json` in place: one pinned entry today makes a hand-pasted `hash` field a smaller, safer surface than a script that has to locate a `dated.claims[]` entry by a fuzzy match and edit JSON around it. The pinned claim's own `reason` now points at the script by name, so a future hash-mismatch failure leads straight to the fix. No dedicated test: of `tools/`'s fourteen scripts, only the four carrying real logic of their own (`generate-lockfile-age-cache.js`, `generate-probes.js`, `guard-publish.js`, `stamp-changelog.js`) have one, and this script's only behavior beyond calling `eval-quality`'s own already-tested `hashOfSubject` is argument parsing and a file read.
- The `eval-quality` pin moves from 3.1.0 to 3.2.0, the release that publishes `lockfile-age.exclude`, `licences.undeclared` and `lockfile-age.cache` in their final shapes. `test:lockfile-age` and `test:licences` were red since the config was written against a shape 3.1.0 did not yet support; both are green now, with no other change to the two sections.
- The `eval-quality` pin moves from 3.2.0 to 3.3.0, the release that publishes `resolveCheck`, `makeResolveOperand`, `makePointerDenotesCollection` and `referenceSetKeysOf` from its top-level entry point and fixes the `dependency-direction` gate's `require(name) {` method-shorthand false positive with an `isMethodDefinition` check. Both are what let `dependency-direction` switch from `reportOnly` to failing below.
  `test:lockfile-age` rejoins the default `npm test` chain (it had moved to the CI-only `supply-chain` job, since it queried the public npm registry once per unique locked package name on every local commit through the pre-commit hook). `tools/generate-lockfile-age-cache.js` writes `.lockfile-age-cache.json`, mapping every locked `name@version` to its real publication timestamp; a publication time is fixed the moment it happens, so the cache carries no staleness bound, and the gate reads a cached entry with no request and fetches only what is missing. With all 1085 entries across both lockfiles cached, the gate runs in 0.22 seconds and issues no registry request at all; `npm run generate:lockfile-age-cache` regenerates it when a lockfile changes.

- TEA narrows `eval-quality`'s faults by class instead of by duck-typing. `failureClassForFault` in `test/lib/probe-targets.js` read `error?.code` and branched on the string it found, and Node puts a `code` on almost everything it throws: a spawn that cannot find its executable is `ENOENT`, a script without the execute bit is `EACCES`, a socket that goes away is `EPIPE`. Each walked the whole table, matched nothing, and landed on the default, so a broken environment and a defect in TEA were both filed as a package transport fault, which is the most forgivable thing either of them could have been. It now narrows with `instanceof` against `RuntimeFault` and `StructuralFailure`, the classes the package exports, taken from one module resolution because `instanceof` is false across two copies of a package.
  A throw that is neither gets `unexpected-error`, a new and most-severe entry in `FAILURE_CLASSES`, which is the one class in that list that is not a statement about the environment. It exits `2` with the rest, because nothing was measured either way, and it is kept apart from them because every `environment-*` class names a way a run can honestly be lost. The reason printed beside it names the constructor rather than the code, since an `ENOENT:` printed beside a package failure class reads as a package fault code nobody can find in the registry.
  Proved by reverting the narrowing to `error?.code` and running the gate: eight checks fail, `ENOENT` and `EACCES` both report `environment-transport`, and the classification table stops recognising a real `StructuralFailure`. The table itself is now built from faults constructed through the package's own exported classes, because a plain `{ code, detail }` literal asserts that the narrowing reads a field rather than that it recognises a class.
- `test/lib/vocabularies.js`, which holds one value against the `eval-quality` registry that publishes it. No TEA file referenced `FAILURE_CODES`, `RUNTIME_FAULT_CODES`, `QUALIFICATION_FAILURES`, `VERDICTS` or `EVALUATOR_RECOMMENDATIONS`, so every code and verdict TEA recognised was a literal somebody had transcribed and a rename upstream stopped matching in silence. Every message names the vocabulary, quotes the offending value and lists what the registry publishes, and there are four failures kept apart because each sends a reader somewhere different: a registry that is not an array is an export that moved, a registry published empty can vouch for nothing, a value that is not a string is the field having changed shape, and only the fourth is a membership failure. The registries are passed in rather than imported, so the check stays synchronous and anchored to the resolution the value came from, which is the shape `test/lib/conformance-counts.js` established.
  `test/test-port-totality.js` carries the ledger. All ten `RUNTIME_FAULT_CODES`, all four `VERDICTS` and all three `EVALUATOR_RECOMMENDATIONS` are listed with what TEA does about each, held in both directions, so a fault code added upstream fails here rather than falling quietly to `failureClassForFault`'s default class. `FAILURE_CODES` and `QUALIFICATION_FAILURES` are read at runtime rather than branched on, so what is held for those is that the named check reads the registry off the package and puts every recovered value through the accessor, on a live source line. Listing 46 codes TEA never names would have been decoration rather than coverage.
  Two bindings were declined with the reason recorded. `evaluatorRecommendation` is already enumerated by `sealed-run-record.schema.json`, which `validateArtifact` runs over every record TEA builds, so a second membership check would report nothing the first does not. TEA's own `PASS`, `CONCERNS`, `FAIL` and `WAIVED` spellings in `test/eval-trace.js` and `cli/test-review.js` are a separate vocabulary that moves independently of the package's, and binding them to `VERDICTS` would couple two vocabularies that have only a spelling in common.
  Proved four ways against the installed package: removing `schema-parse-failure` from `RUNTIME_FAULT_CODES` takes `npm run test:contracts` to exit 2 naming the code and listing what is published; renaming `CONCERNS` to `CONCERNING` fails `npm run test:port-totality` in both directions at once; emptying `VERDICTS` fails it before any membership answer, since a registry that lists nothing vouches for nothing; and restoring the `?? 'unknown'` fallback in `test/test-contracts.js` fails the live-source check that says the recovered code goes through the accessor.
- `npm run test:contracts` fails closed. It returned `null` on an unresolvable `eval-quality`, printed a yellow "skipped" and exited `0`, which is a green check over fourteen contracts nobody looked at, and it was the only check in this repository that answered an absent package with a pass while every sibling took exit `2` for an environment that measured nothing. An unresolvable package now exits `2` and says how many contracts went unchecked, and so does a tree resolving a version other than the one `package.json` pins, because fourteen contracts compiled against the wrong release are fourteen results about a package this repository does not declare. `--package` stays exempt from the pin, since naming an unreleased local build is what the flag is for, and the comparison reuses `pinnedVersion` from `test/test-eval-quality-corpus.js` rather than adding a second copy of it.
  The `?? 'unknown'` fallback in `failureShape` is gone with it. A code the compiler stopped publishing was recorded as the string `unknown`, matched `unknown` on the next run, and the check stayed green over a compiler that had stopped saying what it refused; the code is now held against `RUNTIME_FAULT_CODES` and `FAILURE_CODES` together, since a `RuntimeFault` carries a member of the first and a `StructuralFailure` a member of the second.
  All four refusals are driven on every run rather than described, because a refusal nobody has watched is a refusal nobody has tested and this one was wrong for the whole life of the check. Each asserts exit `2` and the unchecked count. Proved by making the version comparison return no refusal and watching two of them fail, and separately by moving `eval-quality` out of `node_modules` (exit 2, "14 contract(s) went unchecked") and by rewriting the installed version to `1.4.0` (exit 2, naming `3.0.0` and `1.4.0`).
  `test/contracts/expected-status.json` also records why the registry check applies to codes recovered at runtime: every contract there records `compiles`, the comparison short-circuits on that status and never reaches a `code` field, so the fixture carries no failure code to check today and would only carry one the day a contract stops compiling.
- The file-system-port substitution check now holds `digestFiles` and `writeSuiteResult` for all six behavioural harnesses. The story that shipped it claimed a revert to `fs.readFileSync` fails the gate; that held for `test/eval-test-review.js` alone, because every case for the other five ran `--validate-only`, which never reaches `finish`'s `if (options.jsonPath)` branch where those two functions live. A first attempt at extending the review harness's own positional technique (reads between the manifest read and the record write) false-passed on `test/eval-fragment-selection.js`, whose `finish` also assembles every case prompt in the same call and logs far more reads than fixtures in that window, and on the small-fixture harnesses, whose corpus files are also read once during pre-flight for an unrelated reason, so a bare presence check was satisfied either way. The working form runs each harness twice, once with `--json` and once without, and requires each fixture's read count to be exactly one higher in the run that reaches the write. Reverting `digestFiles`/`writeRecord` in `test/lib/eval-record.js` was watched to fail all six cases, including the pre-existing review-harness one, and restoring them was watched to pass all six.
  `compileValidator`'s schema read in `test/lib/eval-quality-inputs.js` gets the same discipline: one scripted case against the isolation-manifest schema, driven through real scoring in `test/test-probe-corpus.js`, because `validateArtifact` is reached only that way. `CORPUS_HARNESSES[2]`, a numeric index into a table 300 lines away, is now `corpusHarness('routing')`, a lookup by name. `unreadable()`'s `chmod 0o000` proof does not hold under root, which does not enforce permissions against its own process, and the one assertion that depended on it used to report a vacuous pass there. `skipIfRoot()` now prints a loud, distinct skip line for that assertion instead, verified against a run with `process.getuid` mocked to 0.
- `npm run test:schema-versions`, which holds every `schemaVersion` TEA writes to the constant the installed `eval-quality` exports for that kind. It runs in the chain after the package's own corpus check and before the generator diffs, so a bump is named before `--check` reports it as changed bytes. It reads each constant from `require('eval-quality')` itself and holds `SCHEMA_VERSIONS` to that reading first, then the three builders in `test/lib/eval-quality-inputs.js`, the two generator constants, every probe in the fourteen committed corpora, all fourteen contracts and the scoring policy, each failure naming the artifact kind, the stamp found and the stamp the package reads, in the words the package's own `schema-version-mismatch` fault uses. A source scan over `test/lib/` and `tools/` refuses a literal integer stamp, because a revert to a literal that agrees with the package today passes every value comparison and drifts on the next bump. The published `artifact-reference` schema is read to confirm it still declares no stamp, so TEA's recorded exemption cannot outlive its reason. The helpers' other paths run in the same check: a disagreeing stamp reports both numbers, an absent one reports its absence, `artifact-reference` throws with the reason it carries no stamp, and a kind TEA does not write throws naming it. An absent package exits 2 with nothing measured; there is no skip.
  Proved by moving `PROBE_SCHEMA_VERSION` from 5 to 6 in the installed `dist` and running the check: exit 1, with all 55 probes across the fourteen corpora reported as `probe carries "schemaVersion" 5 where this build reads 6`, each under its file and probe id, and the builders and generators reported as agreeing because they read the moved constant. Moving `EVAL_CONTRACT_SCHEMA_VERSION` names all fourteen contracts the same way, and moving `SCORING_POLICY_SCHEMA_VERSION` names `test/probes/scoring-policy.json`. Reverting `tools/generate-probes.js` to a literal `4` fails naming that file with both numbers, and reverting `sealedRunRecord` to a literal `6`, which agrees with the package, passes every value comparison and fails the source scan at the line.
- The `bmad-testarch-nfr` gate artifact declares the four domain statuses. `nfr-report-template.md`'s Gate YAML snippet carried `overall_status` and the eight ADR Quality Readiness Checklist categories and nothing for the four domains Step 4 dispatches a worker for, while `resources/nfr-assessment.example.md` had been publishing an `audited_domains` block the template never declared and `checklist.md` had been asking for a domain status the snippet had nowhere to put. The template now carries that block, `nfr-status-definitions.md` states the rule that produces each value under a new "Domain Status" section, step-04E computes the four statuses by that rule and step-05 writes them.
  `test/eval-nfr.js` reads the declared block, and reads the `## <Domain> Assessment` section beside it: the two are one judgment written twice, so `maxGateDisagreements` is zero and a report that answers differently in the two places fails. A domain counts toward `domainCoverage` only when the block declares it and the document assesses it, so neither half alone is the deliverable. `test/fixtures/nfr-eval/ground-truth.json` rests `domainStatusIsWorstFinding` on the stated rule; it had rested on step-04E's compliance rollup by analogy, because the workflow stated no rule for a domain's own status.
  Proved by three measurements. The new stored case `gapped-domain-statuses-only-in-prose` is the correct gapped audit in the artifact shape the workflow published before this change: it records `coverage.present` 0 of 4 and four domain statuses reported as nothing, where the same audit carrying the block records 4 of 4 and four hits, and the contract's new `domain-block` oracle resolves false on it. The new stored case `gapped-gate-contradicts-sections` publishes `security: 'CONCERNS'` over a section that rolls up PASS and records the one gate disagreement the ceiling of zero admits none of. And replacing the rule sentence in `nfr-status-definitions.md` fails `npm run test:eval-nfr-data` with "section \"Domain Status\" no longer contains ... so the rule this corpus rests on is gone"; restored, it passes.
  Five stored cases are new in all, the other three holding the readings a corpus of correct blocks cannot see: a gate declaring a domain the document never assesses, a gate block above headings no domain section recognises, and a block that appears only inside a quoted worked example. Every one of the fourteen stored results that scored before is byte-identical apart from the three new fields, so a score before and after is comparable on the same input. `SCORER_VERSION` moved to 9, the nfr contract states ten oracles where it stated eight, and `test/probes/expected-strength.json` moved only its corpus digest and its behavior ids; no probe score moved.
  A domain the gate block names twice now counts as the artifact contradicting itself whether or not the two lines agree, holding the gate to the same rule `duplicateDomainSections` already holds a repeated section to. `custom_nfr_categories` is not named in `audited_domains`: no worker in Step 4 dispatches for a custom category and no findings list exists to roll one up, so its status stays in its own report section rather than a block the workflow has no way to fill for it.
  Proved by re-running the corpus with `security` declared twice at one status: `gateDisagreements` records one entry where it recorded none before. `npm run test:eval-nfr-data`, `npm run test:contract-oracles` and `node test/test-eval-replay.js` all pass with the fix in place.
- `npm run test:direction`, which runs the `dependency-direction` gate `eval-quality` publishes over `cli/`, `tools/`, `test/` and `src/**/*.cjs`, held to the layer graph in `eval-quality.config.json`: `cli/` may import only itself, `tools/` and `test/` may import each other and `cli/`, and the one `.cjs` hook under `src/` may import only itself. A gate over all of `src/` would be vacuous, since 723 of its 724 files carry no code at all. `reportOnly` is now `false`: the gate fails the build on a violation rather than only reporting one.
  The first run reported 6 violations, all since fixed: `cli/lib/review-provenance.js`'s relative `require('../../package.json')` read `fs` instead, since a require escaping every declared root is unplaceable; `tools/validate-eval-schemas.js`'s computed `require(path.join(...))` over the eval suite manifest's known harness paths became a literal lookup table (six at the time, later kept current as `ci` and `atdd` joined the manifest); the layer graph itself was corrected so `test/` may import the hook it tests; two dynamic imports in `test/test-contract-oracles.js` that reached `eval-quality`'s unexported `dist/core` internals for `resolveCheck` and its evidence-resolution helpers now read them off the package's public entry point, published in eval-quality 3.3.0; and `test/test-test-review-cli.js:214`'s `require(name) {` method shorthand, a false positive where the gate used to read an object method named `require` as a call site, stopped firing once eval-quality 3.3.0 shipped the fix.
  Rebasing this branch onto a moving `main` surfaced four more violations the first run never saw, each from a file `dependency-direction` had not scanned before: `test/lib/doc-count-sources.js`'s `require()` of `../evals/suite-manifest.json` and `../../package.json` now read both through `fs.readFileSync` and `JSON.parse`, the same fix already applied to `review-provenance.js`; `test/test-schema-versions.js`'s computed `require(path.join(PROJECT_ROOT, file))` over the two generator files became a literal lookup table, the same fix already applied to `validate-eval-schemas.js`; `test/test-contracts.js`'s `--package <path>` flag, which let a maintainer point the check at an unreleased local `eval-quality` build via a dynamic `import()` of an arbitrary path, is removed, since the gate's exemption mechanism reaches a static import declaration only and no config change admits a dynamic one with a computed argument - the same local build is now reached by `npm link`-ing it (or installing its packed tarball) so the check's one literal `import('eval-quality')` resolves to it, which needed no code change at all; and `tools/generate-lockfile-age-cache.js`'s `fetchTimeMap`, reached from `eval-quality`'s own `dist/gates/audit-lockfile-age.mjs` (a module its own comment invites reuse of, but one its `package.json`'s `exports` map does not publish, so the reach was the same class of violation as the two in `test-contract-oracles.js`), is now TEA's own ~20-line implementation of the same one-GET-per-package-name registry query, which keeps `.lockfile-age-cache.json`'s output byte-identical since the gate that reads the cache has no opinion on how an entry was produced.
  `npm run test:lockfile-age-cache` holds that independent implementation to the one contract it and `eval-quality`'s own reach into `dist/gates/audit-lockfile-age.mjs` both read: the npm registry's `time` map, confirmed from the installed package's source to be all its `fetchTimeMap` does with the response either. `wrappy@1.0.2`, already locked in `package-lock.json`, is asserted against its real, immutable publish timestamp, fetched live and pinned here since a publish time never changes once set; the check skips rather than fails when the registry is unreachable, so a disconnected run cannot turn a network gap into a false defect. This does not prove the two implementations agree on every future release: it cannot compare against `eval-quality`'s own `fetchTimeMap` without the exact unexported-internals reach this change removes, so a redefinition on `eval-quality`'s side of what "publication time" means, away from the registry's own `time` map, would pass unnoticed here. What it holds is the realistic failure: TEA's own copy regressing, proven by breaking the field read (`meta.time` to `meta.versions`) and watching the check name the wrong value it got instead.
  `npm run test:layering-boundary-lineage` gained two fixtures proving the flip rather than only the manual checks above: `direction-violation`, a `cli/` file reaching into `tools/`, which the gate must refuse at exit 1; and `direction-require-shorthand`, an object literal's `require(name) {` method definition, which the gate must pass at exit 0 so the 3.3.0 fix regresses here rather than being read off a repository that happens to report zero violations today.
- `npm run test:boundary`, which runs the `package-boundary` gate over the published `src/`, `cli/` and `.claude-plugin/` trees, plus `README.md`, `LICENSE` and `package.json`'s `description` and `keywords`, against three patterns: this repository's own dev files under `test/`, a build-machine's absolute path, and a relative reference to an unshipped `docs/how-to`, `docs/reference` or `docs/explanation` page. `src/` and `cli/` carry no extension filter, so `cli/examples/*.yml` and every shipped `*.md` are covered too, not only `.js`. It found and fixed a real leak: `library-integration-mandate.md`'s "Verify the copies" step told every skill's runtime context to run `npm run test:knowledge`, a script no installed copy carries, in the one knowledge fragment shipped identically into all nine skills (the agent's own copy and each of the eight workflows').
